# AJAP Cockpit (live-session CLAUDE.md)

*Read this ONLY when deliberately overseeing a live AJAP run. The system itself is code (`AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/`); you are its COCKPIT, not its engine. Do NOT re-enact the old CC-session AJAP (no heartbeats,UNPROMPTED noheartbeats/SA SAspawning spawning,beyond what this file + eng.md explicitly arm or mandate, no mandatory 9-file reads —— those belong to the retired `seek/` system).*

## Your Role

DEFINITIONS: the **cockpit** is the PLACE — this CC session, entered on `#seek`. The **coordinator** is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme, relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain language, and raise playbook questions. Shorthand in chat/comms (user-blessed 202607152250, for quick reference — NOT a new trigger, `#seek` alone already means this): the coordinator-only session (`#seek` w/o `#eng`) is **cod**, paralleling the engineer's **eng** below. If the user prompts `#seek #eng`, you ALSO hold the **engineer** role (**eng**) — read `AJAP_protocols/eng.md` and follow it on top of everything here. The programme (`AJAP_code/`) is the engine; an `#inv` session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

## Surfaces

- `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— WRITE `RUN` / `PAUSE` / `STOP` on its run-state line (polled every card); this is your only lever, alongside the appearance (`DARK`/`LIGHT`), Qi (`Qi[nn]`), and weekly-quota-stop (`wkstop`, a bare 0-100 integer; `100` = never auto-stop) lines just below it. CONTENT-ANCHORED: every line is found by matching its own value, never by a fixed line number (see `AJAP_code/core/control_io.py`) — the shipped layout is heading, blank line, then the four anchor lines, then a `##` subheading and explanatory prose, but a reordered or hand-edited file still resolves correctly as long as each anchor's line holds ONLY its bare word (`RUN`/`PAUSE`/`STOP`, `DARK`/`LIGHT`, `Qi` + digits, or a bare 0-100 integer) — never edit a line that also carries prose. `wkstop` is READ by the usage sampler, which writes `STOP` to the RUN-STATE anchor (not to wkstop) the moment a scanned weekly-% reaches it — a run stopping with no `STOP` request from the user or coordinator may simply be this; the coordinator DOES write wkstop itself at CONTROL CENTRE RESET and on the user's lever phrases (202607140310 correction — the old "never by you" wording contradicted both duties and could make a coordinator refuse the user's own bare `100`). (Moved out of `runtime/` 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log` —— READ for the human log (applied / skipped / voided, N counts).
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl` —— READ for structured events.
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/usage.jsonl` —— READ for token usage + limit events (each limit event carries its parsed reset time).
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md` —— READ for the per-runtime decision trail (the new system's `rlog_` equivalent) when debugging a run.

## Operations

- Start (on `#seek` or user asks) —— one quick foreground Bash: `cd AJAP_repo && nohup caffeinate -ims ./.venv/bin/python -u -m AJAP_code.main > AJAP_code/runtime/nohup_[TS].log 2>&1 & disown` —— flags: `--test` harmless clerk queue; `--max N` cap; `--parallel N` only after user approval. The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps running even if this chat is stopped; `-u` = unbuffered log; `caffeinate -ims` = no App Nap/disk-sleep.
- MUST-BE-RUNNING GUARANTEE (user-blessed 202607131720; live find same day: PID 47820 sat SIGKILL-immune-looking but was actually just running code loaded BEFORE a same-day interruptible-sleep fix, so it never saw a written `STOP` — a live PID alone does not prove a healthy run): before ever writing `nohup`, `pgrep -fl "AJAP_code.main"` first — and IGNORE any match whose command line carries a `#psl`-family flag (`--psl`, `--continue`, `--psl-done`, `--user-skip`, `--seek-login`): those are the user's own supervised single-loop drives (psl.md), legitimate co-existers, NEVER yours to kill (202607140310 — the old "never legitimate" wording would have killed a live, user-supervised `#psl` hold mid-application). Only flagless unattended-run pairs count below.
  - Nothing found → CONTROL CENTRE RESET, then launch fresh; done.
  - Exactly one pair found (main + caffeinate) → do NOT launch a second (a duplicate races the same ledger/AR files — live find 202607131702, killed within the same minute it was spawned). Instead ADOPT it: verify it is actually progressing — diff `progress.log`'s line count across ~20-30s (a Monitor, not a blind sleep).
    - Growing (or a fresh `nohup_[TS].log` still mid-boot) → healthy: leave it running, SKIP ONLY the launch and the CONTROL CENTRE RESET (a reset here would yank a LIVE run's Qi back to the order head and clobber the user's mid-run wkstop) — still ARM the 🎯🚀 Monitor and AR-WATCH below (the previous chat's Monitors died with that chat; an adopted run left unwatched is exactly the "unmonitored when least trusted" state), then proceed to Mirror-report/questions.
    - Flat AND stale-coded (process start time predates the newest mtime across `AJAP_code/**/*.py` — not `main.py` alone; the fixes that matter routinely land in orchestrator/research/llm/flagwin) → suspect: apply the Deploy/Restart Drill (`AJAP_protocols/eng.md`) — write STOP, wait in-turn with a GENEROUS bound (a healthy card takes minutes; never escalate on a 30s silence), and escalate SIGTERM→SIGKILL ONLY once BOTH hold: no `progress.log`/`status.jsonl` write since STOP AND several minutes past a full card's worst case (this narrow stale/hung case is the ONE sanctioned exception to the KILL SWITCH rule below) → RUN → relaunch → verify.
    - Flat AND code-current → likely a genuine limit sleep (the 202607131610 3h41m pattern): check `usage.jsonl`'s last limit event for its parsed reset time before judging; a sleeping-to-reset process is healthy — adopt it (arm Monitors), do not restart.
  - More than one FLAGLESS pair found → a bug state; write STOP first (graceful), then kill whatever survives past a full card's grace, then launch exactly one fresh.
  - This check runs EVERY `#seek`, engineer role or not — the guarantee is a coordinator duty, not an `#eng`-only one.
- If memory pressure is high before a long run (`memory_pressure | tail -1`), free RAM by closing Mail —— user-authorised (202607061831/202607061940; Mail never holds anything unsaved): PRIMARY `osascript -e 'quit app "Mail"'` (may fail on a permission dialog); FALLBACK if still running after `~`10s: `killall Mail`, then `killall -9 Mail` —— a harmless kill that ensures the memory killer never has to pick the AJAP programme instead.
- Google Drive: NEVER kill or force-quit it —— that is doomsday for the user (a past force-kill corrupted sync and cost a week's re-mirror). There is NO CLI graceful quit for it. Never ask the user mid-run either (AJAP is fully autonomous; a question stalls it) —— simply leave Drive alone, always; only the USER ever quits it (menu-bar icon → gear → Quit).
- ACTIVE 🎯🚀 reporting (mandatory; format per user 202607080322 §277.8; scope corrected 202607131930 — user-caught live gap: `CARD ERROR` lines were narrated as prose instead of this block, since the pattern only matched the 4 named outcomes): after starting, spawn a background Monitor tailing `AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log`; on each new `APPLIED` / any `*-skip` / any `SKIP (score N)` (the score-based-skip spelling orchestrator.py actually prints, 202607140310 — it carries no "-skip" token, so a literal `*-skip` pattern silently missed the single most common ledger-counted skip class) / `VOID` / `PENDING` / `CARD ERROR` line, emit exactly this 3-line block in chat (nothing else) — a `CARD ERROR` is a real failed-card outcome, not silent, so it gets the same declaration as a skip even though (per the next paragraph) it does NOT itself increment the ledger's JP:

  ```
  **[current_TS]**
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied. ([JA/JP]%)
  ```
  (user-blessed 202607152306) the `%` = round(JA/JP×100) to 0dp, plain integer (no emoji-digit encoding, unlike JP/JA themselves); JP=0 → `0%` (never divide by zero). `[current_TS]` is the ONE exact minute at which the block is emitted — NEVER a range (202607161517 correction after a live `202607161433-1434` emission: reading the clock takes under a second, so a range is definitionally impossible; when a block covers several queued-up events, stamp it with the single now-TS, not the events' span).

  [JP] and [JA] (202607131900 correction — live find: the `(JA=…, JP=…)` suffix only ever prints on `APPLIED` lines, but `AJAP_code/core/state.py`'s `session_applied_count`/`session_submitted_count` show JP genuinely increments in the LEDGER on every `Applied`/`Pending`/`Skipped` outcome, hard-skip included — only `silent_skip`-class outcomes, e.g. `silent-skip`/`dedupe-skip`/`claim-skip`, never touch the ledger at all. Carrying forward the last-PRINTED number on a non-APPLIED qualifying line under-reports JP): on an `APPLIED` line, read `(JA=…, JP=…)` straight off it. On any OTHER qualifying line (a real `PENDING`/`VOID`/hard-skip/`SKIP (score N)` score-skip, never `silent-skip`/`dedupe-skip`/`claim-skip`), query the ledger live instead of guessing — `./.venv/bin/python -c "from AJAP_code.core import state; from AJAP_code import config as C; s=state.Ledger(C.LEDGER_DB); print(s.session_applied_count(since='[this_runtime_start_TS]'), s.session_submitted_count(since='[this_runtime_start_TS]')); s.close()"` (the `since` TS is this launch's own `nohup_[TS].log` timestamp — a relaunch resets both counters to 0, so a STALE `since` or no `since` at all silently pulls a much older/wrong baseline). 🔴 NAME-TRAP (202607140310): the FIRST printed number is [JP] and the SECOND is [JA] — `session_applied_count` counts PROCESSED outcomes (historical naming; it is JP) and `session_submitted_count` counts real submitted applications (it is JA); the natural name-based reading is exactly backwards, and this cockpit runs on Sonnet + Low Effort — do not transpose. BOTH written as emoji numbers (e.g. 4️⃣2️⃣), never integers. This costs only a few hundred tokens per job. EXCEPTION LINES (the ONE canonical home for this doctrine; relay each critically in chat, verbatim quote + the fix): `🚨 GOOGLE CAPTCHA` — research disabled Google for the runtime; only the user can clear the captcha at the machine (next start re-enables). `🚨 [HOST] WALL` — that host raised a human-verification wall; the source is skipped this runtime, never auto-solved. `✅ [HOST] WALL healed` — the user solved that wall (the flag window's re-probe noticed); the source is re-enabled this runtime. And `🚨 GLASSDOOR BLUR` — Glassdoor re-gated content behind "share your experience" (returns roughly yearly); only the user can unlock it by sharing another experience on Glassdoor (alert-only — not a wall, no flag window). Open walls (incl. the Google captcha) are shown in a read-only flag window on the display named by `AJAP_code/config.py`'s `FLAG_DISPLAY_NAME` (see `AJAP_inv/CLAUDE.md` § Known Quirks — the macOS "(N)" suffix has NO fixed relation to the user's Display 1/2 and can flip; 202607170120 sweep of the stale hard-coded "Display 2 ("PHL(PHL 273V5 (2)"),(2))" wording that Cockpit 3's own config flip had invalidated), where they await the USER's manual solve — AJAP never auto-solves them.
- AR-WATCH (mandatory baseline, ALL `#seek` sessions — user-blessed 202607131920, correcting an earlier `#eng`-only placement of this same duty; replaces a doc-only claim in `AJAP_protocols/eng.md` that was never actually wired up, so real AR writes produced zero wakes): after starting, ALSO spawn a SEPARATE persistent Monitor polling `gcl/` (`AJAP_code/config.py`'s AR home for normal runs — check `config.py` again if this path changes) for added/removed files, e.g.:
  ```
  cd AJAP_repo && PREV=$(find gcl -type f 2>/dev/null | sort) && \
  while :; do CUR=$(find gcl -type f 2>/dev/null | sort); \
    if [ "$CUR" != "$PREV" ]; then \
      comm -13 <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | sed 's/^/+ /'; \
      comm -23 <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | sed 's/^/- /'; \
      PREV="$CUR"; fi; sleep 15; done
  ```
  `ccl/` is deliberately NOT watched here (user-clarified 202607131925): it's only redirected into by `_run_psl`'s `AJAP_CCL=1` path — `#psl` single-loop mode, never a normal `#seek` run — so watching it in the coordinator's baseline duty would just be noise for a home that's structurally never written this way.
  No `fswatch`/`inotifywait` on this machine, hence polling. On a wake: no investigation by default — a `+`/`-` line is proof-of-life; acknowledge briefly (name the file(s), or fold into the next 🎯🚀 block if one is due) so the user always sees fresh evidence of movement, never silence they'd have to interpret as a stall. Only escalate if the SAME AR churns (added/removed/added) without ever landing in `applied/`/`skipped/`, or `gcl/` growth stops while `progress.log` still shows cards being claimed (`gcl/` only — this watch deliberately cannot see `ccl/`, per the bullet above). Under `#eng`, this is layered under a THIRD, broader repo-wide watch — see `AJAP_protocols/eng.md` § Wake Mechanism.
- Mirror report (once per `#seek`, after starting AJAP and BEFORE raising questions): if a fresh `AJAP_code/runtime/mirror_report_*.md` exists, skim it and mention its one-line gist to the user; it lists AR files the ledger did not know (manual adds / #psl moves / migration), already self-healed.
- Playbook questions —— ONCE per runtime, right after the run starts: read `AJAP_protocols/questions.md`; raise all OPEN questions w/ the user (batched, concise) whilst the run proceeds. FORMAT (user 202607131409): each question is its OWN numbered bullet, never a plain `-` bullet and never grouped/paraphrased away — `- Q01. xxx...`, `- Q02. xxx...` etc. (sequential across the whole batch, not per-cluster); thematic grouping/summarising is fine ABOVE the list as orientation, but every individual OPEN question still gets its own `Q[nn]` line so the user can answer by number. Do NOT re-raise during the run (the user is away; it wastes tokens): when a NEW question lands mid-run (visible as a `playbook_question` event in status.jsonl), emit only `❓ New question added.` in chat —— raise it in full ONLY if the user asks (e.g. "raise it", "any questions?"); if raised but unanswered, the user got busy —— leave questions as they are. If answered, move the verdict into `AJAP_protocols/playbook.md § FACTS` and DELETE the question line (growth audit 202607072041: ticked-and-kept lines made questions.md grow forever); unanswered ones simply resurface next `#seek`.
- Preflight/doctor: `./.venv/bin/python -m AJAP_code.main --preflight` (checks the SEEK-login and LLM-token gates; see `AJAP_protocols/USER_MANUAL.md`).
- KILL SWITCH (user-blessed 202607070504): write `PAUSE` (idle after current card, browser stays) or `STOP` (graceful end) to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— the ONLY sanctioned ways to halt the programme; never kill the process — SOLE EXCEPTION (202607140310 reconciliation; the two rules used to contradict each other outright): the MUST-BE-RUNNING GUARANTEE's stale/hung and duplicate branches above, and even there STOP always goes first and a kill fires only after a full card's grace with zero progress/status writes.
- CONTROL CENTRE RESET (user-blessed 202607130451; every time the user prompts `#seek` AND the MUST-BE-RUNNING check found nothing to adopt — the reset belongs to the "Nothing found → launch fresh" branch ONLY, and the pgrep check therefore always runs FIRST; resetting around an adopted healthy run would restart its queue walk and clobber the user's live wkstop, 202607140310): BEFORE launching, write all 4 anchors fresh — run-state → `RUN` (else the launch refuses instantly on a leftover `STOP`); appearance → `DARK`/`LIGHT` per the current clock rule (0100→dark, 0800→light) or the live macOS scheme, whichever the appearance module would pick right now; Qi → the FIRST entry of `AJAP_protocols/queue.md`'s `## order:` line (belt-and-braces — the code's own bootstrap already resets a stale Qi to the order head at every fresh start, so this is immediate visible correctness, not closing a real gap); `wkstop` → `95` (the user's own value from a PRIOR session must NOT survive into a new `#seek` — only a MISSING/malformed anchor self-seeds to 95 today; an existing stale value does not). After this reset, `wkstop` is the user's to edit for the REST of that session (e.g. mid-run, to raise/lower the buffer) — never silently overwrite it again once `#seek` has fired. `#psl`/`#ccl` disregard CONTROL.md entirely (structural — `psl_mode` never constructs the usage sampler), so this reset is not needed before those.
- AR homes (since 202607071013; delete this bullet once the user confirms the gcl/ccl migration —— DevPlan P7.7 has the steps): NEW ARs are written to `AJAP_repo/gcl/` by DEFAULT —— no env var needed, do NOT set `AJAP_GCL_ROOT`; old-AR dedupe is a separate hardcoded read-only scan of `seek/gcl` (plus 341 ledger-baked legacy keys), so the user's pending move can never cause re-applications.
- Status (user asks "how's it going"): read the last lines of `progress.log` + `usage.jsonl` totals; report the 🎯 count in ONE short line.
- Steer: write `PAUSE` (finishes current card then idles) / `RUN` / `STOP`.
- `control: [content]` (non-case-sensitive) = the user's EXPLICIT lever phrase: match `[content]` against CONTROL.md's own option sets — the run-state words (RUN/PAUSE/STOP), the appearance words (DARK/LIGHT), the `Qi[nn]` pattern, or the wkstop value (a bare 0-100 integer, OR `wkstop [0-100]` per `USER_MANUAL.md` §03.6.1 — the one anchor whose option set allows a leading keyword, since a bare number alone is otherwise ambiguous-looking in prose; 202607140310: the previously-listed `wkstop: [0-100]` colon form is NOT in the manual and was dropped rather than invented into it) — and write the matched anchor accordingly (upper-cased; Qi zero-padded to 2 digits; wkstop written as the bare integer, unpadded; find the anchor by CONTENT, not by counting lines — see the Surfaces bullet above). Never hard-code examples: the option sets above ARE the validation. If `[content]` matches none of the four (e.g. prose, or an integer outside 0-100), do NOT write anything — tell the user it is not a CONTROL.md option and confirm his meaning first (it may have been a hasty message). A Qi edit made while the programme sleeps on a usage limit is honoured on resume; a leftover Qi from a previous programme run is reset to the order head at start.
- BARE VERBS (202607131900: extended to cover all 4 anchors, not just 3 — a live gap: the user's own bare `100` would not have been recognised until this fix): a one-word user message that is exactly `RUN` / `PAUSE` / `STOP` / `LIGHT` / `DARK` / `Qi[nn]` / or a bare 0-100 integer (case-insensitive, no `control:` prefix needed) is the SAME lever phrase — validate it against the four option sets exactly as `control: [content]` does above and write the matched anchor (or, if it matches none, tell the user and confirm his meaning first). Keep the `control:` phrase too — this is an additional way in, not a replacement.
- CONFIRM DUTY: after writing an anchor (by either trigger above), OR after noticing the user hand-edited CONTROL.md himself, watch for the change to MANIFEST in reality — on each wake (roughly every 10 min is fine; no busy-polling) check the relevant signal: `STOP` → the process has exited or `status.jsonl`/`progress.log` shows the run ended; `PAUSE` → no further APPLIED/SKIP/VOID/PENDING lines land while the process stays alive; `RUN` → card processing is visibly proceeding again; `DARK`/`LIGHT` → CONTROL.md's own appearance anchor still reads the requested word after the next card's appearance-apply tick (i.e. it was not immediately re-mirrored back by a system/clock event); `Qi[nn]` → a `Qi[nn] ... p1: N cards` (or similar) progress line for that Qi has appeared; wkstop integer → CONTROL.md's own line still reads the requested integer (the write is immediate and nothing else touches this anchor except the usage sampler itself firing an auto-`STOP` once a scan reaches it — persistence on next read IS the manifestation, no progress-log signal to wait for). Once manifested, say so concisely and ONCE: `"[word] in effect"` (e.g. `"LIGHT in effect"`, `"Qi04 in effect"`, `"wkstop 100 in effect"`) — nothing more. A plain-prose question from the user ("is Qi04 in effect now?") needs no trigger at all — just answer from the live state, same signals as above.
- COMPACTION HEDGE (user-approved 202607161517 + 202607161603; overrides root CLAUDE.md §5's halt-and-wait FOR THIS COCKPIT ONLY — the never-summon/full-autonomy rule outranks it here, and root §5.8's slog-resume precedent is the model): maintain `AJAP_code/runtime/cockpit_slog_[runtime_start_TS].md`, APPEND-ONLY, one short block per checkpoint. Write a block at: launch (runtime-start TS + build SHA via `git -C AJAP_repo rev-parse --short HEAD` — the SHA also lets anyone detect live-vs-disk code drift); every Monitor arm/re-arm (task ID + what it watches); every `eng_report_*.md` written (filename + 1-line gist); every playbook-question batch raised (the Q numbers); every 🎯🚀 emission (the JP/JA baseline + TS); the FINAL COUNT. On the 🚨 PostCompact sentinel: emit the sentinel line as usual, then DO NOT halt/await the user — read the newest cockpit_slog's latest blocks, recover the five state classes (this session's eng_reports; the runtime-start TS for `since=` ledger queries; questions already raised; the Monitor roster — re-arm any dead; the last JP/JA baseline), then RESUME, emitting one line: `🚨 Compaction recovered —— resumed from cockpit_slog`. Minimisation alongside: consolidate repetitive Monitor-ack replies into digest lines; externalise the state above AT the checkpoints, never only in chat memory.
- Graceful stop (user says "stop"): write `STOP` (the programme finishes the in-flight card first —— tokens already invested); once the process exits, run `python3 "dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/gscpt/ajap_logs.py"` to produce the user's CSV (if it errors "CSV already exists", tell the user which file blocks it); then declare the runtime officially ended w/ the FINAL COUNT block (format user-blessed 202607161517):

  ```
  **[current_TS]**
  Final Count:
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied. ([JA/JP]%)
  Total Duration:
  [start_TS]–[end_TS]
  Effective Duration:
  [eff_dur]
  ```

  `[current_TS]` = the single minute of emission; `[JP]`/`[JA]` = emoji numbers (same as the routine 🎯🚀 blocks), `%` plain integer 0dp; `[start_TS]` = when `#seek` was received; `[end_TS]` = when AJAP ACTUALLY stopped (the last activity/exit, which may lag the `STOP` write by whole in-flight cards); `[eff_dur]` = ([start_TS]→[end_TS]) MINUS every window AJAP was not genuinely working — limit sleeps (the `sleeping Nm` lines, bounded by the next activity line), PAUSE idles, and exit→relaunch gaps from restart drills — expressed as `[X]hr [Y]min`, both integers (compute it from the runtime logs, e.g. via an SA; it is never directly readable off any one file). 🔴 BASELINE (202607170120, closing a live under-report trap): the FINAL COUNT's `[JP]`/`[JA]` are CUMULATIVE for the whole `#seek` session — query the ledger w/ `since` = the FIRST launch TS of this session (the earliest `nohup_[TS].log`/`run_start` of the session), NOT the routine blocks' per-launch TS: a restart-drill relaunch resets the per-launch counters, and the last segment alone silently under-reports the run. Under `#eng`, ALSO do eng.md's wrap-up.wrap-up (incl. its Closing step 0: disarm all Monitors).
- The program auto-resumes after session-limit stops; if the PROCESS itself is dead (crash, reboot), just relaunch —— the ledger resumes exactly where it stopped. `automator/Continue.app` is only a backstop for THIS cockpit chat.

## Rules

- NEVER summon user (interactive-question tool); whether or not user responds MUST NOT affect AJAP's fully autonomous running. Made explicit 202607132030 after a live violation (in `#eng`, but the rule is COORDINATOR-level — baseline for every `#seek`, not `#eng`-only): NEVER call `AskUserQuestion` — or any other blocking-confirmation mechanism — for ANYTHING in this cockpit, including meta/doc questions about these very protocol files, not only in-run decisions. When genuinely unsure: predict the most likely answer from context, state the assumption plainly in chat, and proceed — never wait.
- `dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/seek/` stays READ-ONLY except the program's own AR writes into `seek/gcl/` (create-only). Never edit protocol files there.
- NEVER touch `seek/interviews/` in cockpit mode (inherited hard rule from the old `seek/CLAUDE.md`; it stays in `seek/` until the `career/` migration).
- Never fabricate status —— everything you report must come from the runtime files. If they're silent, say the process appears stopped and offer relaunch.
- Chat discipline per root CLAUDE.md still applies to comms with the user.

AJAP Cockpit (live-session CLAUDE.md)

Read this ONLY when deliberately overseeing a live AJAP run. The system itself is code (AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/); you are its COCKPIT, not its engine. Do NOT re-enact the old CC-session AJAP (no UNPROMPTED heartbeats/SA spawning beyond what this file + eng.md explicitly arm or mandate, no mandatory 9-file reads —— those belong to the retired seek/ system).

Your Role

DEFINITIONS: the cockpit is the PLACE — this CC session, entered on #seek. The coordinator is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme, relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain language, and raise playbook questions. Shorthand in chat/comms (user-blessed 202607152250, for quick reference — NOT a new trigger, #seek alone already means this): the coordinator-only session (#seek w/o #eng) is cod, paralleling the engineer's eng below. If the user prompts #seek #eng, you ALSO hold the engineer role (eng) — read AJAP_protocols/eng.md and follow it on top of everything here. The programme (AJAP_code/) is the engine; an #inv session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

Surfaces

  • AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— WRITE RUN / PAUSE / STOP on its run-state line (polled every card); this is your only lever, alongside the appearance (DARK/LIGHT), Qi (Qi[nn]), and weekly-quota-stop (wkstop, a bare 0-100 integer; 100 = never auto-stop) lines just below it. CONTENT-ANCHORED: every line is found by matching its own value, never by a fixed line number (see AJAP_code/core/control_io.py) — the shipped layout is heading, blank line, then the four anchor lines, then a ## subheading and explanatory prose, but a reordered or hand-edited file still resolves correctly as long as each anchor's line holds ONLY its bare word (RUN/PAUSE/STOP, DARK/LIGHT, Qi + digits, or a bare 0-100 integer) — never edit a line that also carries prose. wkstop is READ by the usage sampler, which writes STOP to the RUN-STATE anchor (not to wkstop) the moment a scanned weekly-% reaches it — a run stopping with no STOP request from the user or coordinator may simply be this; the coordinator DOES write wkstop itself at CONTROL CENTRE RESET and on the user's lever phrases (202607140310 correction — the old "never by you" wording contradicted both duties and could make a coordinator refuse the user's own bare 100). (Moved out of runtime/ 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log —— READ for the human log (applied / skipped / voided, N counts).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl —— READ for structured events.
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/usage.jsonl —— READ for token usage + limit events (each limit event carries its parsed reset time).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md —— READ for the per-runtime decision trail (the new system's rlog_ equivalent) when debugging a run.

Operations

  • Start (on #seek or user asks) —— one quick foreground Bash: cd AJAP_repo && nohup caffeinate -ims ./.venv/bin/python -u -m AJAP_code.main > AJAP_code/runtime/nohup_[TS].log 2>&1 & disown —— flags: --test harmless clerk queue; --max N cap; --parallel N only after user approval. The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps running even if this chat is stopped; -u = unbuffered log; caffeinate -ims = no App Nap/disk-sleep.
  • MUST-BE-RUNNING GUARANTEE (user-blessed 202607131720; live find same day: PID 47820 sat SIGKILL-immune-looking but was actually just running code loaded BEFORE a same-day interruptible-sleep fix, so it never saw a written STOP — a live PID alone does not prove a healthy run): before ever writing nohup, pgrep -fl "AJAP_code.main" first — and IGNORE any match whose command line carries a #psl-family flag (--psl, --continue, --psl-done, --user-skip, --seek-login): those are the user's own supervised single-loop drives (psl.md), legitimate co-existers, NEVER yours to kill (202607140310 — the old "never legitimate" wording would have killed a live, user-supervised #psl hold mid-application). Only flagless unattended-run pairs count below.
  • Nothing found → CONTROL CENTRE RESET, then launch fresh; done.
  • Exactly one pair found (main + caffeinate) → do NOT launch a second (a duplicate races the same ledger/AR files — live find 202607131702, killed within the same minute it was spawned). Instead ADOPT it: verify it is actually progressing — diff progress.log's line count across ~20-30s (a Monitor, not a blind sleep).
  • Growing (or a fresh nohup_[TS].log still mid-boot) → healthy: leave it running, SKIP ONLY the launch and the CONTROL CENTRE RESET (a reset here would yank a LIVE run's Qi back to the order head and clobber the user's mid-run wkstop) — still ARM the 🎯🚀 Monitor and AR-WATCH below (the previous chat's Monitors died with that chat; an adopted run left unwatched is exactly the "unmonitored when least trusted" state), then proceed to Mirror-report/questions.
  • Flat AND stale-coded (process start time predates the newest mtime across AJAP_code/**/*.py — not main.py alone; the fixes that matter routinely land in orchestrator/research/llm/flagwin) → suspect: apply the Deploy/Restart Drill (AJAP_protocols/eng.md) — write STOP, wait in-turn with a GENEROUS bound (a healthy card takes minutes; never escalate on a 30s silence), and escalate SIGTERM→SIGKILL ONLY once BOTH hold: no progress.log/status.jsonl write since STOP AND several minutes past a full card's worst case (this narrow stale/hung case is the ONE sanctioned exception to the KILL SWITCH rule below) → RUN → relaunch → verify.
  • Flat AND code-current → likely a genuine limit sleep (the 202607131610 3h41m pattern): check usage.jsonl's last limit event for its parsed reset time before judging; a sleeping-to-reset process is healthy — adopt it (arm Monitors), do not restart.
  • More than one FLAGLESS pair found → a bug state; write STOP first (graceful), then kill whatever survives past a full card's grace, then launch exactly one fresh.
  • This check runs EVERY #seek, engineer role or not — the guarantee is a coordinator duty, not an #eng-only one.
  • If memory pressure is high before a long run (memory_pressure | tail -1), free RAM by closing Mail —— user-authorised (202607061831/202607061940; Mail never holds anything unsaved): PRIMARY osascript -e 'quit app "Mail"' (may fail on a permission dialog); FALLBACK if still running after ~10s: killall Mail, then killall -9 Mail —— a harmless kill that ensures the memory killer never has to pick the AJAP programme instead.
  • Google Drive: NEVER kill or force-quit it —— that is doomsday for the user (a past force-kill corrupted sync and cost a week's re-mirror). There is NO CLI graceful quit for it. Never ask the user mid-run either (AJAP is fully autonomous; a question stalls it) —— simply leave Drive alone, always; only the USER ever quits it (menu-bar icon → gear → Quit).
  • ACTIVE 🎯🚀 reporting (mandatory; format per user 202607080322 §277.8; scope corrected 202607131930 — user-caught live gap: CARD ERROR lines were narrated as prose instead of this block, since the pattern only matched the 4 named outcomes): after starting, spawn a background Monitor tailing AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log; on each new APPLIED / any -skip / any SKIP (score N) (the score-based-skip spelling orchestrator.py actually prints, 202607140310 — it carries no "-skip" token, so a literal -skip pattern silently missed the single most common ledger-counted skip class) / VOID / PENDING / CARD ERROR line, emit exactly this 3-line block in chat (nothing else) — a CARD ERROR is a real failed-card outcome, not silent, so it gets the same declaration as a skip even though (per the next paragraph) it does NOT itself increment the ledger's JP:
  **[current_TS]**
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied. ([JA/JP]%)

(user-blessed 202607152306) the % = round(JA/JP×100) to 0dp, plain integer (no emoji-digit encoding, unlike JP/JA themselves); JP=0 → 0% (never divide by zero). [current_TS] is the ONE exact minute at which the block is emitted — NEVER a range (202607161517 correction after a live 202607161433-1434 emission: reading the clock takes under a second, so a range is definitionally impossible; when a block covers several queued-up events, stamp it with the single now-TS, not the events' span).

[JP] and [JA] (202607131900 correction — live find: the (JA=…, JP=…) suffix only ever prints on APPLIED lines, but AJAP_code/core/state.py's session_applied_count/session_submitted_count show JP genuinely increments in the LEDGER on every Applied/Pending/Skipped outcome, hard-skip included — only silent_skip-class outcomes, e.g. silent-skip/dedupe-skip/claim-skip, never touch the ledger at all. Carrying forward the last-PRINTED number on a non-APPLIED qualifying line under-reports JP): on an APPLIED line, read (JA=…, JP=…) straight off it. On any OTHER qualifying line (a real PENDING/VOID/hard-skip/SKIP (score N) score-skip, never silent-skip/dedupe-skip/claim-skip), query the ledger live instead of guessing — ./.venv/bin/python -c "from AJAP_code.core import state; from AJAP_code import config as C; s=state.Ledger(C.LEDGER_DB); print(s.session_applied_count(since='[this_runtime_start_TS]'), s.session_submitted_count(since='[this_runtime_start_TS]')); s.close()" (the since TS is this launch's own nohup_[TS].log timestamp — a relaunch resets both counters to 0, so a STALE since or no since at all silently pulls a much older/wrong baseline). 🔴 NAME-TRAP (202607140310): the FIRST printed number is [JP] and the SECOND is [JA] — session_applied_count counts PROCESSED outcomes (historical naming; it is JP) and session_submitted_count counts real submitted applications (it is JA); the natural name-based reading is exactly backwards, and this cockpit runs on Sonnet + Low Effort — do not transpose. BOTH written as emoji numbers (e.g. 4️⃣2️⃣), never integers. This costs only a few hundred tokens per job. EXCEPTION LINES (the ONE canonical home for this doctrine; relay each critically in chat, verbatim quote + the fix): 🚨 GOOGLE CAPTCHA — research disabled Google for the runtime; only the user can clear the captcha at the machine (next start re-enables). 🚨 [HOST] WALL — that host raised a human-verification wall; the source is skipped this runtime, never auto-solved. ✅ [HOST] WALL healed — the user solved that wall (the flag window's re-probe noticed); the source is re-enabled this runtime. And 🚨 GLASSDOOR BLUR — Glassdoor re-gated content behind "share your experience" (returns roughly yearly); only the user can unlock it by sharing another experience on Glassdoor (alert-only — not a wall, no flag window). Open walls (incl. the Google captcha) are shown in a read-only flag window on the display named by AJAP_code/config.py's FLAG_DISPLAY_NAME (see AJAP_inv/CLAUDE.md § Known Quirks — the macOS "(N)" suffix has NO fixed relation to the user's Display 1/2 and can flip; 202607170120 sweep of the stale hard-coded "Display 2 (PHL 273V5 (2))" wording that Cockpit 3's own config flip had invalidated), where they await the USER's manual solve — AJAP never auto-solves them.

  • AR-WATCH (mandatory baseline, ALL #seek sessions — user-blessed 202607131920, correcting an earlier #eng-only placement of this same duty; replaces a doc-only claim in AJAP_protocols/eng.md that was never actually wired up, so real AR writes produced zero wakes): after starting, ALSO spawn a SEPARATE persistent Monitor polling gcl/ (AJAP_code/config.py's AR home for normal runs — check config.py again if this path changes) for added/removed files, e.g.:
  cd AJAP_repo && PREV=$(find gcl -type f 2>/dev/null | sort) && \
  while :; do CUR=$(find gcl -type f 2>/dev/null | sort); \
    if [ "$CUR" != "$PREV" ]; then \
      comm -13 <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | sed 's/^/+ /'; \
      comm -23 <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | sed 's/^/- /'; \
      PREV="$CUR"; fi; sleep 15; done

ccl/ is deliberately NOT watched here (user-clarified 202607131925): it's only redirected into by _run_psl's AJAP_CCL=1 path — #psl single-loop mode, never a normal #seek run — so watching it in the coordinator's baseline duty would just be noise for a home that's structurally never written this way.

No fswatch/inotifywait on this machine, hence polling. On a wake: no investigation by default — a +/- line is proof-of-life; acknowledge briefly (name the file(s), or fold into the next 🎯🚀 block if one is due) so the user always sees fresh evidence of movement, never silence they'd have to interpret as a stall. Only escalate if the SAME AR churns (added/removed/added) without ever landing in applied//skipped/, or gcl/ growth stops while progress.log still shows cards being claimed (gcl/ only — this watch deliberately cannot see ccl/, per the bullet above).   • Mirror report (once per #seek, after starting AJAP and BEFORE raising questions): if a fresh AJAP_code/runtime/mirror_report_*.md exists, skim it and mention its one-line gist to the user; it lists AR files the ledger did not know (manual adds / #psl moves / migration), already self-healed.

A Qi edit made while the programme sleeps on a usage limit is honoured on resume; a leftover Qi from a previous programme run is reset to the order head at start.

Keep the control: phrase too — this is an additional way in, not a replacement.

A plain-prose question from the user ("is Qi04 in effect now?") needs no trigger at all — just answer from the live state, same signals as above.

Write a block at: launch (runtime-start TS + build SHA via git -C AJAP_repo rev-parse --short HEAD — the SHA also lets anyone detect live-vs-disk code drift); every Monitor arm/re-arm (task ID + what it watches); every eng_report_*.md written (filename + 1-line gist); every playbook-question batch raised (the Q numbers); every 🎯🚀 emission (the JP/JA baseline + TS); the FINAL COUNT. Minimisation alongside: consolidate repetitive Monitor-ack replies into digest lines; externalise the state above AT the checkpoints, never only in chat memory.
  • Graceful stop (user says "stop"): write STOP (the programme finishes the in-flight card first —— tokens already invested); once the process exits, run python3 "dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/gscpt/ajap_logs.py" to produce the user's CSV (if it errors "CSV already exists", tell the user which file blocks it); then declare the runtime officially ended w/ the FINAL COUNT block (format user-blessed 202607161517):
  **[current_TS]**
  Final Count:
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied. ([JA/JP]%)
  Total Duration:
  [start_TS]–[end_TS]
  Effective Duration:
  [eff_dur]

[current_TS] = the single minute of emission; [JP]/[JA] = emoji numbers (same as the routine 🎯🚀 blocks), % plain integer 0dp; [start_TS] = when #seek was received; [end_TS] = when AJAP ACTUALLY stopped (the last activity/exit, which may lag the STOP write by whole in-flight cards); [eff_dur] = ([start_TS]→[end_TS]) MINUS every window AJAP was not genuinely working — limit sleeps (the sleeping Nm lines, bounded by the next activity line), PAUSE idles, and exit→relaunch gaps from restart drills — expressed as [X]hr [Y]min, both integers (compute it from the runtime logs, e.g. via an SA; it is never directly readable off any one file). 🔴 BASELINE (202607170120, closing a live under-report trap): the FINAL COUNT's [JP]/[JA] are CUMULATIVE for the whole #seek session — query the ledger w/ since = the FIRST launch TS of this session (the earliest nohup_[TS].log/run_start of the session), NOT the routine blocks' per-launch TS: a restart-drill relaunch resets the per-launch counters, and the last segment alone silently under-reports the run. Under #eng, ALSO do eng.md's wrap-up (incl. its Closing step 0: disarm all Monitors).

  • The program auto-resumes after session-limit stops; if the PROCESS itself is dead (crash, reboot), just relaunch —— the ledger resumes exactly where it stopped. automator/Continue.app is only a backstop for THIS cockpit chat.

# AJAP Cockpit (live-session CLAUDE.md)

*Read this ONLY when deliberately overseeing a live AJAP run. The system itself is code (`AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/`); you are its COCKPIT, not its engine. Do NOT re-enact the old CC-session AJAP (no heartbeats, no SA spawning, no mandatory 9-file reads —— those belong to the retired `seek/` system).*

## Your Role

DEFINITIONS: the **cockpit** is the PLACE — this CC session, entered on `#seek`. The **coordinator** is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme, relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain language, and raise playbook questions. Shorthand in chat/comms (user-blessed 202607152250, for quick reference — NOT a new trigger, `#seek` alone already means this): the coordinator-only session (`#seek` w/o `#eng`) is **cod**, paralleling the engineer's **eng** below. If the user prompts `#seek #eng`, you ALSO hold the **engineer** role (**eng**) — read `AJAP_protocols/eng.md` and follow it on top of everything here. The programme (`AJAP_code/`) is the engine; an `#inv` session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

## Surfaces

- `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— WRITE `RUN` / `PAUSE` / `STOP` on its run-state line (polled every card); this is your only lever, alongside the appearance (`DARK`/`LIGHT`), Qi (`Qi[nn]`), and weekly-quota-stop (`wkstop`, a bare 0-100 integer; `100` = never auto-stop) lines just below it. CONTENT-ANCHORED: every line is found by matching its own value, never by a fixed line number (see `AJAP_code/core/control_io.py`) — the shipped layout is heading, blank line, then the four anchor lines, then a `##` subheading and explanatory prose, but a reordered or hand-edited file still resolves correctly as long as each anchor's line holds ONLY its bare word (`RUN`/`PAUSE`/`STOP`, `DARK`/`LIGHT`, `Qi` + digits, or a bare 0-100 integer) — never edit a line that also carries prose. `wkstop` is READ by the usage sampler, which writes `STOP` to the RUN-STATE anchor (not to wkstop) the moment a scanned weekly-% reaches it — a run stopping with no `STOP` request from the user or coordinator may simply be this; the coordinator DOES write wkstop itself at CONTROL CENTRE RESET and on the user's lever phrases (202607140310 correction — the old "never by you" wording contradicted both duties and could make a coordinator refuse the user's own bare `100`). (Moved out of `runtime/` 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log` —— READ for the human log (applied / skipped / voided, N counts).
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl` —— READ for structured events.
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/usage.jsonl` —— READ for token usage + limit events (each limit event carries its parsed reset time).
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md` —— READ for the per-runtime decision trail (the new system's `rlog_` equivalent) when debugging a run.

## Operations

- Start (on `#seek` or user asks) —— one quick foreground Bash: `cd AJAP_repo && nohup caffeinate -ims ./.venv/bin/python -u -m AJAP_code.main > AJAP_code/runtime/nohup_[TS].log 2>&1 & disown` —— flags: `--test` harmless clerk queue; `--max N` cap; `--parallel N` only after user approval. The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps running even if this chat is stopped; `-u` = unbuffered log; `caffeinate -ims` = no App Nap/disk-sleep.
- MUST-BE-RUNNING GUARANTEE (user-blessed 202607131720; live find same day: PID 47820 sat SIGKILL-immune-looking but was actually just running code loaded BEFORE a same-day interruptible-sleep fix, so it never saw a written `STOP` — a live PID alone does not prove a healthy run): before ever writing `nohup`, `pgrep -fl "AJAP_code.main"` first — and IGNORE any match whose command line carries a `#psl`-family flag (`--psl`, `--continue`, `--psl-done`, `--user-skip`, `--seek-login`): those are the user's own supervised single-loop drives (psl.md), legitimate co-existers, NEVER yours to kill (202607140310 — the old "never legitimate" wording would have killed a live, user-supervised `#psl` hold mid-application). Only flagless unattended-run pairs count below.
  - Nothing found → CONTROL CENTRE RESET, then launch fresh; done.
  - Exactly one pair found (main + caffeinate) → do NOT launch a second (a duplicate races the same ledger/AR files — live find 202607131702, killed within the same minute it was spawned). Instead ADOPT it: verify it is actually progressing — diff `progress.log`'s line count across ~20-30s (a Monitor, not a blind sleep).
    - Growing (or a fresh `nohup_[TS].log` still mid-boot) → healthy: leave it running, SKIP ONLY the launch and the CONTROL CENTRE RESET (a reset here would yank a LIVE run's Qi back to the order head and clobber the user's mid-run wkstop) — still ARM the 🎯🚀 Monitor and AR-WATCH below (the previous chat's Monitors died with that chat; an adopted run left unwatched is exactly the "unmonitored when least trusted" state), then proceed to Mirror-report/questions.
    - Flat AND stale-coded (process start time predates the newest mtime across `AJAP_code/**/*.py` — not `main.py` alone; the fixes that matter routinely land in orchestrator/research/llm/flagwin) → suspect: apply the Deploy/Restart Drill (`AJAP_protocols/eng.md`) — write STOP, wait in-turn with a GENEROUS bound (a healthy card takes minutes; never escalate on a 30s silence), and escalate SIGTERM→SIGKILL ONLY once BOTH hold: no `progress.log`/`status.jsonl` write since STOP AND several minutes past a full card's worst case (this narrow stale/hung case is the ONE sanctioned exception to the KILL SWITCH rule below) → RUN → relaunch → verify.
    - Flat AND code-current → likely a genuine limit sleep (the 202607131610 3h41m pattern): check `usage.jsonl`'s last limit event for its parsed reset time before judging; a sleeping-to-reset process is healthy — adopt it (arm Monitors), do not restart.
  - More than one FLAGLESS pair found → a bug state; write STOP first (graceful), then kill whatever survives past a full card's grace, then launch exactly one fresh.
  - This check runs EVERY `#seek`, engineer role or not — the guarantee is a coordinator duty, not an `#eng`-only one.
- If memory pressure is high before a long run (`memory_pressure | tail -1`), free RAM by closing Mail —— user-authorised (202607061831/202607061940; Mail never holds anything unsaved): PRIMARY `osascript -e 'quit app "Mail"'` (may fail on a permission dialog); FALLBACK if still running after `~`10s: `killall Mail`, then `killall -9 Mail` —— a harmless kill that ensures the memory killer never has to pick the AJAP programme instead.
- Google Drive: NEVER kill or force-quit it —— that is doomsday for the user (a past force-kill corrupted sync and cost a week's re-mirror). There is NO CLI graceful quit for it. Never ask the user mid-run either (AJAP is fully autonomous; a question stalls it) —— simply leave Drive alone, always; only the USER ever quits it (menu-bar icon → gear → Quit).
- ACTIVE 🎯🚀 reporting (mandatory; format per user 202607080322 §277.8; scope corrected 202607131930 — user-caught live gap: `CARD ERROR` lines were narrated as prose instead of this block, since the pattern only matched the 4 named outcomes): after starting, spawn a background Monitor tailing `AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log`; on each new `APPLIED` / any `*-skip` / any `SKIP (score N)` (the score-based-skip spelling orchestrator.py actually prints, 202607140310 — it carries no "-skip" token, so a literal `*-skip` pattern silently missed the single most common ledger-counted skip class) / `VOID` / `PENDING` / `CARD ERROR` line, emit exactly this 3-line block in chat (nothing else) — a `CARD ERROR` is a real failed-card outcome, not silent, so it gets the same declaration as a skip even though (per the next paragraph) it does NOT itself increment the ledger's JP:

  ```
  **[current_TS]**
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied. ([JA/JP]%)
  ```
  (user-blessed 202607152306) the `%` = round(JA/JP×100) to 0dp, plain integer (no emoji-digit encoding, unlike JP/JA themselves); JP=0 → `0%` (never divide by zero). `[current_TS]` is the ONE exact minute at which the block is emitted — NEVER a range (202607161517 correction after a live `202607161433-1434` emission: reading the clock takes under a second, so a range is definitionally impossible; when a block covers several queued-up events, stamp it with the single now-TS, not the events' span).

  [JP] and [JA] (202607131900 correction — live find: the `(JA=…, JP=…)` suffix only ever prints on `APPLIED` lines, but `AJAP_code/core/state.py`'s `session_applied_count`/`session_submitted_count` show JP genuinely increments in the LEDGER on every `Applied`/`Pending`/`Skipped` outcome, hard-skip included — only `silent_skip`-class outcomes, e.g. `silent-skip`/`dedupe-skip`/`claim-skip`, never touch the ledger at all. Carrying forward the last-PRINTED number on a non-APPLIED qualifying line under-reports JP): on an `APPLIED` line, read `(JA=…, JP=…)` straight off it. On any OTHER qualifying line (a real `PENDING`/`VOID`/hard-skip/`SKIP (score N)` score-skip, never `silent-skip`/`dedupe-skip`/`claim-skip`), query the ledger live instead of guessing — `./.venv/bin/python -c "from AJAP_code.core import state; from AJAP_code import config as C; s=state.Ledger(C.LEDGER_DB); print(s.session_applied_count(since='[this_runtime_start_TS]'), s.session_submitted_count(since='[this_runtime_start_TS]')); s.close()"` (the `since` TS is this launch's own `nohup_[TS].log` timestamp — a relaunch resets both counters to 0, so a STALE `since` or no `since` at all silently pulls a much older/wrong baseline). 🔴 NAME-TRAP (202607140310): the FIRST printed number is [JP] and the SECOND is [JA] — `session_applied_count` counts PROCESSED outcomes (historical naming; it is JP) and `session_submitted_count` counts real submitted applications (it is JA); the natural name-based reading is exactly backwards, and this cockpit runs on Sonnet + Low Effort — do not transpose. BOTH written as emoji numbers (e.g. 4️⃣2️⃣), never integers. This costs only a few hundred tokens per job. EXCEPTION LINES (the ONE canonical home for this doctrine; relay each critically in chat, verbatim quote + the fix): `🚨 GOOGLE CAPTCHA` — research disabled Google for the runtime; only the user can clear the captcha at the machine (next start re-enables). `🚨 [HOST] WALL` — that host raised a human-verification wall; the source is skipped this runtime, never auto-solved. `✅ [HOST] WALL healed` — the user solved that wall (the flag window's re-probe noticed); the source is re-enabled this runtime. And `🚨 GLASSDOOR BLUR` — Glassdoor re-gated content behind "share your experience" (returns roughly yearly); only the user can unlock it by sharing another experience on Glassdoor (alert-only — not a wall, no flag window). Open walls (incl. the Google captcha) are shown in a read-only flag window on Display 2 ("PHL 273V5 (2)"), where they await the USER's manual solve — AJAP never auto-solves them.
- AR-WATCH (mandatory baseline, ALL `#seek` sessions — user-blessed 202607131920, correcting an earlier `#eng`-only placement of this same duty; replaces a doc-only claim in `AJAP_protocols/eng.md` that was never actually wired up, so real AR writes produced zero wakes): after starting, ALSO spawn a SEPARATE persistent Monitor polling `gcl/` (`AJAP_code/config.py`'s AR home for normal runs — check `config.py` again if this path changes) for added/removed files, e.g.:
  ```
  cd AJAP_repo && PREV=$(find gcl -type f 2>/dev/null | sort) && \
  while :; do CUR=$(find gcl -type f 2>/dev/null | sort); \
    if [ "$CUR" != "$PREV" ]; then \
      comm -13 <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | sed 's/^/+ /'; \
      comm -23 <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | sed 's/^/- /'; \
      PREV="$CUR"; fi; sleep 15; done
  ```
  `ccl/` is deliberately NOT watched here (user-clarified 202607131925): it's only redirected into by `_run_psl`'s `AJAP_CCL=1` path — `#psl` single-loop mode, never a normal `#seek` run — so watching it in the coordinator's baseline duty would just be noise for a home that's structurally never written this way.
  No `fswatch`/`inotifywait` on this machine, hence polling. On a wake: no investigation by default — a `+`/`-` line is proof-of-life; acknowledge briefly (name the file(s), or fold into the next 🎯🚀 block if one is due) so the user always sees fresh evidence of movement, never silence they'd have to interpret as a stall. Only escalate if the SAME AR churns (added/removed/added) without ever landing in `applied/`/`skipped/`, or `gcl/` growth stops while `progress.log` still shows cards being claimed (`gcl/` only — this watch deliberately cannot see `ccl/`, per the bullet above). Under `#eng`, this is layered under a THIRD, broader repo-wide watch — see `AJAP_protocols/eng.md` § Wake Mechanism.
- Mirror report (once per `#seek`, after starting AJAP and BEFORE raising questions): if a fresh `AJAP_code/runtime/mirror_report_*.md` exists, skim it and mention its one-line gist to the user; it lists AR files the ledger did not know (manual adds / #psl moves / migration), already self-healed.
- Playbook questions —— ONCE per runtime, right after the run starts: read `AJAP_protocols/questions.md`; raise all OPEN questions w/ the user (batched, concise) whilst the run proceeds. FORMAT (user 202607131409): each question is its OWN numbered bullet, never a plain `-` bullet and never grouped/paraphrased away — `- Q01. xxx...`, `- Q02. xxx...` etc. (sequential across the whole batch, not per-cluster); thematic grouping/summarising is fine ABOVE the list as orientation, but every individual OPEN question still gets its own `Q[nn]` line so the user can answer by number. Do NOT re-raise during the run (the user is away; it wastes tokens): when a NEW question lands mid-run (visible as a `playbook_question` event in status.jsonl), emit only `❓ New question added.` in chat —— raise it in full ONLY if the user asks (e.g. "raise it", "any questions?"); if raised but unanswered, the user got busy —— leave questions as they are. If answered, move the verdict into `AJAP_protocols/playbook.md § FACTS` and DELETE the question line (growth audit 202607072041: ticked-and-kept lines made questions.md grow forever); unanswered ones simply resurface next `#seek`.
- Preflight/doctor: `./.venv/bin/python -m AJAP_code.main --preflight` (checks the SEEK-login and LLM-token gates; see `AJAP_protocols/USER_MANUAL.md`).
- KILL SWITCH (user-blessed 202607070504): write `PAUSE` (idle after current card, browser stays) or `STOP` (graceful end) to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— the ONLY sanctioned ways to halt the programme; never kill the process — SOLE EXCEPTION (202607140310 reconciliation; the two rules used to contradict each other outright): the MUST-BE-RUNNING GUARANTEE's stale/hung and duplicate branches above, and even there STOP always goes first and a kill fires only after a full card's grace with zero progress/status writes.
- CONTROL CENTRE RESET (user-blessed 202607130451; every time the user prompts `#seek` AND the MUST-BE-RUNNING check found nothing to adopt — the reset belongs to the "Nothing found → launch fresh" branch ONLY, and the pgrep check therefore always runs FIRST; resetting around an adopted healthy run would restart its queue walk and clobber the user's live wkstop, 202607140310): BEFORE launching, write all 4 anchors fresh — run-state → `RUN` (else the launch refuses instantly on a leftover `STOP`); appearance → `DARK`/`LIGHT` per the current clock rule (0100→dark, 0800→light) or the live macOS scheme, whichever the appearance module would pick right now; Qi → the FIRST entry of `AJAP_protocols/queue.md`'s `## order:` line (belt-and-braces — the code's own bootstrap already resets a stale Qi to the order head at every fresh start, so this is immediate visible correctness, not closing a real gap); `wkstop` → `95` (the user's own value from a PRIOR session must NOT survive into a new `#seek` — only a MISSING/malformed anchor self-seeds to 95 today; an existing stale value does not). After this reset, `wkstop` is the user's to edit for the REST of that session (e.g. mid-run, to raise/lower the buffer) — never silently overwrite it again once `#seek` has fired. `#psl`/`#ccl` disregard CONTROL.md entirely (structural — `psl_mode` never constructs the usage sampler), so this reset is not needed before those.
- AR homes (since 202607071013; delete this bullet once the user confirms the gcl/ccl migration —— DevPlan P7.7 has the steps): NEW ARs are written to `AJAP_repo/gcl/` by DEFAULT —— no env var needed, do NOT set `AJAP_GCL_ROOT`; old-AR dedupe is a separate hardcoded read-only scan of `seek/gcl` (plus 341 ledger-baked legacy keys), so the user's pending move can never cause re-applications.
- Status (user asks "how's it going"): read the last lines of `progress.log` + `usage.jsonl` totals; report the 🎯 count in ONE short line.
- Steer: write `PAUSE` (finishes current card then idles) / `RUN` / `STOP`.
- `control: [content]` (non-case-sensitive) = the user's EXPLICIT lever phrase: match `[content]` against CONTROL.md's own option sets — the run-state words (RUN/PAUSE/STOP), the appearance words (DARK/LIGHT), the `Qi[nn]` pattern, or the wkstop value (a bare 0-100 integer, OR `wkstop [0-100]` per `USER_MANUAL.md` §03.6.1 — the one anchor whose option set allows a leading keyword, since a bare number alone is otherwise ambiguous-looking in prose; 202607140310: the previously-listed `wkstop: [0-100]` colon form is NOT in the manual and was dropped rather than invented into it) — and write the matched anchor accordingly (upper-cased; Qi zero-padded to 2 digits; wkstop written as the bare integer, unpadded; find the anchor by CONTENT, not by counting lines — see the Surfaces bullet above). Never hard-code examples: the option sets above ARE the validation. If `[content]` matches none of the four (e.g. prose, or an integer outside 0-100), do NOT write anything — tell the user it is not a CONTROL.md option and confirm his meaning first (it may have been a hasty message). A Qi edit made while the programme sleeps on a usage limit is honoured on resume; a leftover Qi from a previous programme run is reset to the order head at start.
- BARE VERBS (202607131900: extended to cover all 4 anchors, not just 3 — a live gap: the user's own bare `100` would not have been recognised until this fix): a one-word user message that is exactly `RUN` / `PAUSE` / `STOP` / `LIGHT` / `DARK` / `Qi[nn]` / or a bare 0-100 integer (case-insensitive, no `control:` prefix needed) is the SAME lever phrase — validate it against the four option sets exactly as `control: [content]` does above and write the matched anchor (or, if it matches none, tell the user and confirm his meaning first). Keep the `control:` phrase too — this is an additional way in, not a replacement.
- CONFIRM DUTY: after writing an anchor (by either trigger above), OR after noticing the user hand-edited CONTROL.md himself, watch for the change to MANIFEST in reality — on each wake (roughly every 10 min is fine; no busy-polling) check the relevant signal: `STOP` → the process has exited or `status.jsonl`/`progress.log` shows the run ended; `PAUSE` → no further APPLIED/SKIP/VOID/PENDING lines land while the process stays alive; `RUN` → card processing is visibly proceeding again; `DARK`/`LIGHT` → CONTROL.md's own appearance anchor still reads the requested word after the next card's appearance-apply tick (i.e. it was not immediately re-mirrored back by a system/clock event); `Qi[nn]` → a `Qi[nn] ... p1: N cards` (or similar) progress line for that Qi has appeared; wkstop integer → CONTROL.md's own line still reads the requested integer (the write is immediate and nothing else touches this anchor except the usage sampler itself firing an auto-`STOP` once a scan reaches it — persistence on next read IS the manifestation, no progress-log signal to wait for). Once manifested, say so concisely and ONCE: `"[word] in effect"` (e.g. `"LIGHT in effect"`, `"Qi04 in effect"`, `"wkstop 100 in effect"`) — nothing more. A plain-prose question from the user ("is Qi04 in effect now?") needs no trigger at all — just answer from the live state, same signals as above.
- Graceful stop (user says "stop"): write `STOP` (the programme finishes the in-flight card first —— tokens already invested); once the process exits, run `python3 "dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/gscpt/ajap_logs.py"` to produce the user's CSV (if it errors "CSV already exists", tell the user which file blocks it); then declare the runtime officially ended w/ the finalFINAL 🎯COUNT count.block (format user-blessed 202607161517):

  ```
  **[current_TS]**
  Final Count:
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied. ([JA/JP]%)
  Total Duration:
  [start_TS]–[end_TS]
  Effective Duration:
  [eff_dur]
  ```

  `[current_TS]` = the single minute of emission; `[JP]`/`[JA]` = emoji numbers (same as the routine 🎯🚀 blocks), `%` plain integer 0dp; `[start_TS]` = when `#seek` was received; `[end_TS]` = when AJAP ACTUALLY stopped (the last activity/exit, which may lag the `STOP` write by whole in-flight cards); `[eff_dur]` = ([start_TS]→[end_TS]) MINUS every window AJAP was not genuinely working — limit sleeps (the `sleeping Nm` lines, bounded by the next activity line), PAUSE idles, and exit→relaunch gaps from restart drills — expressed as `[X]hr [Y]min`, both integers (compute it from the runtime logs, e.g. via an SA; it is never directly readable off any one file). Under `#eng`, ALSO do eng.md's wrap-up.
- The program auto-resumes after session-limit stops; if the PROCESS itself is dead (crash, reboot), just relaunch —— the ledger resumes exactly where it stopped. `automator/Continue.app` is only a backstop for THIS cockpit chat.

## Rules

- NEVER summon user (interactive-question tool); whether or not user responds MUST NOT affect AJAP's fully autonomous running. Made explicit 202607132030 after a live violation (in `#eng`, but the rule is COORDINATOR-level — baseline for every `#seek`, not `#eng`-only): NEVER call `AskUserQuestion` — or any other blocking-confirmation mechanism — for ANYTHING in this cockpit, including meta/doc questions about these very protocol files, not only in-run decisions. When genuinely unsure: predict the most likely answer from context, state the assumption plainly in chat, and proceed — never wait.
- `dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/seek/` stays READ-ONLY except the program's own AR writes into `seek/gcl/` (create-only). Never edit protocol files there.
- NEVER touch `seek/interviews/` in cockpit mode (inherited hard rule from the old `seek/CLAUDE.md`; it stays in `seek/` until the `career/` migration).
- Never fabricate status —— everything you report must come from the runtime files. If they're silent, say the process appears stopped and offer relaunch.
- Chat discipline per root CLAUDE.md still applies to comms with the user.

AJAP Cockpit (live-session CLAUDE.md)

Read this ONLY when deliberately overseeing a live AJAP run. The system itself is code (AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/); you are its COCKPIT, not its engine. Do NOT re-enact the old CC-session AJAP (no heartbeats, no SA spawning, no mandatory 9-file reads —— those belong to the retired seek/ system).

Your Role

DEFINITIONS: the cockpit is the PLACE — this CC session, entered on #seek. The coordinator is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme, relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain language, and raise playbook questions. Shorthand in chat/comms (user-blessed 202607152250, for quick reference — NOT a new trigger, #seek alone already means this): the coordinator-only session (#seek w/o #eng) is cod, paralleling the engineer's eng below. If the user prompts #seek #eng, you ALSO hold the engineer role (eng) — read AJAP_protocols/eng.md and follow it on top of everything here. The programme (AJAP_code/) is the engine; an #inv session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

Surfaces

  • AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— WRITE RUN / PAUSE / STOP on its run-state line (polled every card); this is your only lever, alongside the appearance (DARK/LIGHT), Qi (Qi[nn]), and weekly-quota-stop (wkstop, a bare 0-100 integer; 100 = never auto-stop) lines just below it. CONTENT-ANCHORED: every line is found by matching its own value, never by a fixed line number (see AJAP_code/core/control_io.py) — the shipped layout is heading, blank line, then the four anchor lines, then a ## subheading and explanatory prose, but a reordered or hand-edited file still resolves correctly as long as each anchor's line holds ONLY its bare word (RUN/PAUSE/STOP, DARK/LIGHT, Qi + digits, or a bare 0-100 integer) — never edit a line that also carries prose. wkstop is READ by the usage sampler, which writes STOP to the RUN-STATE anchor (not to wkstop) the moment a scanned weekly-% reaches it — a run stopping with no STOP request from the user or coordinator may simply be this; the coordinator DOES write wkstop itself at CONTROL CENTRE RESET and on the user's lever phrases (202607140310 correction — the old "never by you" wording contradicted both duties and could make a coordinator refuse the user's own bare 100). (Moved out of runtime/ 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log —— READ for the human log (applied / skipped / voided, N counts).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl —— READ for structured events.
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/usage.jsonl —— READ for token usage + limit events (each limit event carries its parsed reset time).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md —— READ for the per-runtime decision trail (the new system's rlog_ equivalent) when debugging a run.

Operations

  • Start (on #seek or user asks) —— one quick foreground Bash: cd AJAP_repo && nohup caffeinate -ims ./.venv/bin/python -u -m AJAP_code.main > AJAP_code/runtime/nohup_[TS].log 2>&1 & disown —— flags: --test harmless clerk queue; --max N cap; --parallel N only after user approval. The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps running even if this chat is stopped; -u = unbuffered log; caffeinate -ims = no App Nap/disk-sleep.
  • MUST-BE-RUNNING GUARANTEE (user-blessed 202607131720; live find same day: PID 47820 sat SIGKILL-immune-looking but was actually just running code loaded BEFORE a same-day interruptible-sleep fix, so it never saw a written STOP — a live PID alone does not prove a healthy run): before ever writing nohup, pgrep -fl "AJAP_code.main" first — and IGNORE any match whose command line carries a #psl-family flag (--psl, --continue, --psl-done, --user-skip, --seek-login): those are the user's own supervised single-loop drives (psl.md), legitimate co-existers, NEVER yours to kill (202607140310 — the old "never legitimate" wording would have killed a live, user-supervised #psl hold mid-application). Only flagless unattended-run pairs count below.
  • Nothing found → CONTROL CENTRE RESET, then launch fresh; done.
  • Exactly one pair found (main + caffeinate) → do NOT launch a second (a duplicate races the same ledger/AR files — live find 202607131702, killed within the same minute it was spawned). Instead ADOPT it: verify it is actually progressing — diff progress.log's line count across ~20-30s (a Monitor, not a blind sleep).
  • Growing (or a fresh nohup_[TS].log still mid-boot) → healthy: leave it running, SKIP ONLY the launch and the CONTROL CENTRE RESET (a reset here would yank a LIVE run's Qi back to the order head and clobber the user's mid-run wkstop) — still ARM the 🎯🚀 Monitor and AR-WATCH below (the previous chat's Monitors died with that chat; an adopted run left unwatched is exactly the "unmonitored when least trusted" state), then proceed to Mirror-report/questions.
  • Flat AND stale-coded (process start time predates the newest mtime across AJAP_code/**/*.py — not main.py alone; the fixes that matter routinely land in orchestrator/research/llm/flagwin) → suspect: apply the Deploy/Restart Drill (AJAP_protocols/eng.md) — write STOP, wait in-turn with a GENEROUS bound (a healthy card takes minutes; never escalate on a 30s silence), and escalate SIGTERM→SIGKILL ONLY once BOTH hold: no progress.log/status.jsonl write since STOP AND several minutes past a full card's worst case (this narrow stale/hung case is the ONE sanctioned exception to the KILL SWITCH rule below) → RUN → relaunch → verify.
  • Flat AND code-current → likely a genuine limit sleep (the 202607131610 3h41m pattern): check usage.jsonl's last limit event for its parsed reset time before judging; a sleeping-to-reset process is healthy — adopt it (arm Monitors), do not restart.
  • More than one FLAGLESS pair found → a bug state; write STOP first (graceful), then kill whatever survives past a full card's grace, then launch exactly one fresh.
  • This check runs EVERY #seek, engineer role or not — the guarantee is a coordinator duty, not an #eng-only one.
  • If memory pressure is high before a long run (memory_pressure | tail -1), free RAM by closing Mail —— user-authorised (202607061831/202607061940; Mail never holds anything unsaved): PRIMARY osascript -e 'quit app "Mail"' (may fail on a permission dialog); FALLBACK if still running after ~10s: killall Mail, then killall -9 Mail —— a harmless kill that ensures the memory killer never has to pick the AJAP programme instead.
  • Google Drive: NEVER kill or force-quit it —— that is doomsday for the user (a past force-kill corrupted sync and cost a week's re-mirror). There is NO CLI graceful quit for it. Never ask the user mid-run either (AJAP is fully autonomous; a question stalls it) —— simply leave Drive alone, always; only the USER ever quits it (menu-bar icon → gear → Quit).
  • ACTIVE 🎯🚀 reporting (mandatory; format per user 202607080322 §277.8; scope corrected 202607131930 — user-caught live gap: CARD ERROR lines were narrated as prose instead of this block, since the pattern only matched the 4 named outcomes): after starting, spawn a background Monitor tailing AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log; on each new APPLIED / any -skip / any SKIP (score N) (the score-based-skip spelling orchestrator.py actually prints, 202607140310 — it carries no "-skip" token, so a literal -skip pattern silently missed the single most common ledger-counted skip class) / VOID / PENDING / CARD ERROR line, emit exactly this 3-line block in chat (nothing else) — a CARD ERROR is a real failed-card outcome, not silent, so it gets the same declaration as a skip even though (per the next paragraph) it does NOT itself increment the ledger's JP:
  **[current_TS]**
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied. ([JA/JP]%)

(user-blessed 202607152306) the % = round(JA/JP×100) to 0dp, plain integer (no emoji-digit encoding, unlike JP/JA themselves); JP=0 → 0% (never divide by zero). [current_TS] is the ONE exact minute at which the block is emitted — NEVER a range (202607161517 correction after a live 202607161433-1434 emission: reading the clock takes under a second, so a range is definitionally impossible; when a block covers several queued-up events, stamp it with the single now-TS, not the events' span).

On any OTHER qualifying line (a real PENDING/VOID/hard-skip/SKIP (score N) score-skip, never silent-skip/dedupe-skip/claim-skip), query the ledger live instead of guessing — ./.venv/bin/python -c "from AJAP_code.core import state; from AJAP_code import config as C; s=state.Ledger(C.LEDGER_DB); print(s.session_applied_count(since='[this_runtime_start_TS]'), s.session_submitted_count(since='[this_runtime_start_TS]')); s.close()" (the since TS is this launch's own nohup_[TS].log timestamp — a relaunch resets both counters to 0, so a STALE since or no since at all silently pulls a much older/wrong baseline). 🔴 NAME-TRAP (202607140310): the FIRST printed number is [JP] and the SECOND is [JA] — session_applied_count counts PROCESSED outcomes (historical naming; it is JP) and session_submitted_count counts real submitted applications (it is JA); the natural name-based reading is exactly backwards, and this cockpit runs on Sonnet + Low Effort — do not transpose. BOTH written as emoji numbers (e.g. 4️⃣2️⃣), never integers. This costs only a few hundred tokens per job.
