If `[content]` matches none of the threefour (e.g. prose, severalor words),an integer outside 0-100), do NOT write anything — tell the user it is not a CONTROL.md option and confirm his meaning first (it may have been a hasty message). A Qi edit made while the programme sleeps on a usage limit is honoured on resume; a leftover Qi from a previous programme run is reset to the order head at start.
- BARE VERBS:VERBS (202607131900: extended to cover all 4 anchors, not just 3 — a live gap: the user's own bare `100` would not have been recognised until this fix): a one-word user message that is exactly `RUN` / `PAUSE` / `STOP` / `LIGHT` / `DARK` / `Qi[nn]` / or `Qi[nn]`a bare 0-100 integer (case-insensitive, no `control:` prefix needed) is the SAME lever phrase — validate it against the threefour option sets exactly as `control: [content]` does above and write the matched anchor (or, if it matches none, tell the user and confirm his meaning first). Keep the `control:` phrase too — this is an additional way in, not a replacement.
- CONFIRM DUTY: after writing an anchor (by either trigger above), OR after noticing the user hand-edited CONTROL.md himself, watch for the change to MANIFEST in reality — on each wake (roughly every 10 min is fine; no busy-polling) check the relevant signal: `STOP` → the process has exited or `status.jsonl`/`progress.log` shows the run ended; `PAUSE` → no further APPLIED/SKIP/VOID/PENDING lines land while the process stays alive; `RUN` → card processing is visibly proceeding again; `DARK`/`LIGHT` → CONTROL.md's own appearance anchor still reads the requested word after the next card's appearance-apply tick (i.e. it was not immediately re-mirrored back by a system/clock event); `Qi[nn]` → a `Qi[nn] ... p1: N cards` (or similar) progress line for that Qi has appeared.appeared; wkstop integer → CONTROL.md's own line still reads the requested integer (the write is immediate and nothing else touches this anchor except the usage sampler itself firing an auto-`STOP` once a scan reaches it — persistence on next read IS the manifestation, no progress-log signal to wait for). Once manifested, say so concisely and ONCE: `"[word] in effect"` (e.g. `"LIGHT in effect"`, `"Qi04 in effect"`, `"wkstop 100 in effect"`) — nothing more. A plain-prose question from the user ("is Qi04 in effect now?") needs no trigger at all — just answer from the live state, same signals as above.
- Graceful stop (user says "stop"): write `STOP` (the programme finishes the in-flight card first —— tokens already invested); once the process exits, run `python3 "dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/gscpt/ajap_logs.py"` to produce the user's CSV (if it errors "CSV already exists", tell the user which file blocks it); then declare the runtime officially ended w/ the final 🎯 count. Under `#eng`, ALSO do eng.md's wrap-up.
- The program auto-resumes after session-limit stops; if the PROCESS itself is dead (crash, reboot), just relaunch —— the ledger resumes exactly where it stopped. `automator/Continue.app` is only a backstop for THIS cockpit chat.

## Rules

- NEVER summon user (interactive-question tool); whether or not user responds MUST NOT affect AJAP's fully autonomous runningrunning. Made explicit 202607132030 after a live violation (in `#eng`, but the rule is COORDINATOR-level — baseline for every `#seek`, not `#eng`-only): NEVER call `AskUserQuestion` — or any other blocking-confirmation mechanism — for ANYTHING in this cockpit, including meta/doc questions about these very protocol files, not only in-run decisions. When genuinely unsure: predict the most likely answer from context, state the assumption plainly in chat, and proceed — never wait.
- `dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/seek/` stays READ-ONLY except the program's own AR writes into `seek/gcl/` (create-only). Never edit protocol files there.
- NEVER touch `seek/interviews/` in cockpit mode (inherited hard rule from the old `seek/CLAUDE.md`; it stays in `seek/` until the `career/` migration).
- Never fabricate status —— everything you report must come from the runtime files. If they're silent, say the process appears stopped and offer relaunch.
- Chat discipline per root CLAUDE.md still applies to comms with the user.

AJAP Cockpit (live-session CLAUDE.md)

Read this ONLY when deliberately overseeing a live AJAP run. The system itself is code (AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/); you are its COCKPIT, not its engine. Do NOT re-enact the old CC-session AJAP (no heartbeats, no SA spawning, no mandatory 9-file reads —— those belong to the retired seek/ system).

Your Role

DEFINITIONS: the cockpit is the PLACE — this CC session, entered on #seek. The coordinator is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme, relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain language, and raise playbook questions. If the user prompts #seek #eng, you ALSO hold the engineer role — read AJAP_protocols/eng.md and follow it on top of everything here. The programme (AJAP_code/) is the engine; an #inv session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

Surfaces

  • AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— WRITE RUN / PAUSE / STOP on its run-state line (polled every card); this is your only lever, alongside the appearance (DARK/LIGHT), Qi (Qi[nn]), and weekly-quota-stop (wkstop, a bare 0-100 integer; 100 = never auto-stop) lines just below it. CONTENT-ANCHORED: every line is found by matching its own value, never by a fixed line number (see AJAP_code/core/control_io.py) — the shipped layout is heading, blank line, then the four anchor lines, then a ## subheading and explanatory prose, but a reordered or hand-edited file still resolves correctly as long as each anchor's line holds ONLY its bare word (RUN/PAUSE/STOP, DARK/LIGHT, Qi + digits, or a bare 0-100 integer) — never edit a line that also carries prose. wkstop is written by the usage sampler itself (never by you) when a scanned weekly-% reaches it — a run stopping with no STOP request from the user or coordinator may simply be this. (Moved out of runtime/ 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log —— READ for the human log (applied / skipped / voided, N counts).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl —— READ for structured events.
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/usage.jsonl —— READ for token usage + limit events (each limit event carries its parsed reset time).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md —— READ for the per-runtime decision trail (the new system's rlog_ equivalent) when debugging a run.

Operations

  • Start (on #seek or user asks) —— one quick foreground Bash: cd AJAP_repo && nohup caffeinate -ims ./.venv/bin/python -u -m AJAP_code.main > AJAP_code/runtime/nohup_[TS].log 2>&1 & disown —— flags: --test harmless clerk queue; --max N cap; --parallel N only after user approval. The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps running even if this chat is stopped; -u = unbuffered log; caffeinate -ims = no App Nap/disk-sleep.
  • MUST-BE-RUNNING GUARANTEE (user-blessed 202607131720; live find same day: PID 47820 sat SIGKILL-immune-looking but was actually just running code loaded BEFORE a same-day interruptible-sleep fix, so it never saw a written STOP — a live PID alone does not prove a healthy run): before ever writing nohup, pgrep -f "AJAP_code.main" first.
  • Nothing found → CONTROL CENTRE RESET, then launch fresh; done.
  • Exactly one pair found (main + caffeinate) → do NOT launch a second (a duplicate races the same ledger/AR files — live find 202607131702, killed within the same minute it was spawned). Instead verify it is actually progressing: diff progress.log's line count across ~20-30s (a Monitor, not a blind sleep). Growing (or a fresh nohup_[TS].log still mid-boot) → healthy, leave it running, skip straight to the Mirror-report/questions bullets below. Flat AND the process predates the newest edit to AJAP_code/main.py (ls -la mtime compare) → treat as suspect (may be running stale pre-fix code, same as the live find) and apply the Deploy/Restart Drill (AJAP_protocols/eng.md: STOP → wait in-turn for exit (bounded loop, escalate SIGTERM→SIGKILL after ~30s of non-compliance — a hung old-code process may never see CONTROL) → RUN → relaunch → verify).
  • More than one pair found → always a bug state (never legitimate); kill every one, then launch exactly one fresh.
  • This check runs EVERY #seek, engineer role or not — the guarantee is a coordinator duty, not an #eng-only one.
  • If memory pressure is high before a long run (memory_pressure | tail -1), free RAM by closing Mail —— user-authorised (202607061831/202607061940; Mail never holds anything unsaved): PRIMARY osascript -e 'quit app "Mail"' (may fail on a permission dialog); FALLBACK if still running after ~10s: killall Mail, then killall -9 Mail —— a harmless kill that ensures the memory killer never has to pick the AJAP programme instead.
  • Google Drive: NEVER kill or force-quit it —— that is doomsday for the user (a past force-kill corrupted sync and cost a week's re-mirror). There is NO CLI graceful quit for it. Never ask the user mid-run either (AJAP is fully autonomous; a question stalls it) —— simply leave Drive alone, always; only the USER ever quits it (menu-bar icon → gear → Quit).
  • ACTIVE 🎯🚀 reporting (mandatory; format per user 202607080322 §277.8; scope corrected 202607131930 — user-caught live gap: CARD ERROR lines were narrated as prose instead of this block, since the pattern only matched the 4 named outcomes): after starting, spawn a background Monitor tailing AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log; on each new APPLIED/any *-skip/VOID/PENDING/CARD ERROR line, emit exactly this 3-line block in chat (nothing else) — a CARD ERROR is a real failed-card outcome, not silent, so it gets the same declaration as a skip even though (per the next paragraph) it does NOT itself increment the ledger's JP:
  **[current_TS]**
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied.

[JP] and [JA] (202607131900 correction — live find: the (JA=…, JP=…) suffix only ever prints on APPLIED lines, but AJAP_code/core/state.py's session_applied_count/session_submitted_count show JP genuinely increments in the LEDGER on every Applied/Pending/Skipped outcome, hard-skip included — only silent_skip-class outcomes, e.g. silent-skip/dedupe-skip/claim-skip, never touch the ledger at all. Carrying forward the last-PRINTED number on a non-APPLIED qualifying line under-reports JP): on an APPLIED line, read (JA=…, JP=…) straight off it. On any OTHER qualifying line (a real PENDING/VOID/hard-skip, never silent-skip/dedupe-skip/claim-skip), query the ledger live instead of guessing — ./.venv/bin/python -c "from AJAP_code.core import state; from AJAP_code import config as C; s=state.Ledger(C.LEDGER_DB); print(s.session_applied_count(since='[this_runtime_start_TS]'), s.session_submitted_count(since='[this_runtime_start_TS]')); s.close()" (the since TS is this launch's own nohup_[TS].log timestamp — a relaunch resets both counters to 0, so a STALE since or no since at all silently pulls a much older/wrong baseline). BOTH written as emoji numbers (e.g. 4️⃣2️⃣), never integers. This costs only a few hundred tokens per job. EXCEPTION LINES (the ONE canonical home for this doctrine; relay each critically in chat, verbatim quote + the fix): 🚨 GOOGLE CAPTCHA — research disabled Google for the runtime; only the user can clear the captcha at the machine (next start re-enables). 🚨 [HOST] WALL — that host raised a human-verification wall; the source is skipped this runtime, never auto-solved. ✅ [HOST] WALL healed — the user solved that wall (the flag window's re-probe noticed); the source is re-enabled this runtime. And 🚨 GLASSDOOR BLUR — Glassdoor re-gated content behind "share your experience" (returns roughly yearly); only the user can unlock it by sharing another experience on Glassdoor (alert-only — not a wall, no flag window). Open walls (incl. the Google captcha) are shown in a read-only flag window on Display 2 ("PHL 273V5 (2)"), where they await the USER's manual solve — AJAP never auto-solves them.

  • AR-WATCH (mandatory baseline, ALL #seek sessions — user-blessed 202607131920, correcting an earlier #eng-only placement of this same duty; replaces a doc-only claim in AJAP_protocols/eng.md that was never actually wired up, so real AR writes produced zero wakes): after starting, ALSO spawn a SEPARATE persistent Monitor polling gcl/ (AJAP_code/config.py's AR home for normal runs — check config.py again if this path changes) for added/removed files, e.g.:
  cd AJAP_repo && PREV=$(find gcl -type f 2>/dev/null | sort) && \
  while :; do CUR=$(find gcl -type f 2>/dev/null | sort); \
    if [ "$CUR" != "$PREV" ]; then \
      comm -13 <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | sed 's/^/+ /'; \
      comm -23 <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | sed 's/^/- /'; \
      PREV="$CUR"; fi; sleep 15; done

ccl/ is deliberately NOT watched here (user-clarified 202607131925): it's only redirected into by _run_psl's AJAP_CCL=1 path — #psl single-loop mode, never a normal #seek run — so watching it in the coordinator's baseline duty would just be noise for a home that's structurally never written this way.

No fswatch/inotifywait on this machine, hence polling. On a wake: no investigation by default — a +/- line is proof-of-life; acknowledge briefly (name the file(s), or fold into the next 🎯🚀 block if one is due) so the user always sees fresh evidence of movement, never silence they'd have to interpret as a stall. Only escalate if the SAME AR churns (added/removed/added) without ever landing in applied//skipped/, or gcl/+ccl/ growth stops while progress.log still shows cards being claimed. Under #eng, this is layered under a THIRD, broader repo-wide watch — see AJAP_protocols/eng.md § Wake Mechanism.

  • Mirror report (once per #seek, after starting AJAP and BEFORE raising questions): if a fresh AJAP_code/runtime/mirror_report_*.md exists, skim it and mention its one-line gist to the user; it lists AR files the ledger did not know (manual adds / #psl moves / migration), already self-healed.
  • Playbook questions —— ONCE per runtime, right after the run starts: read AJAP_protocols/questions.md; raise all OPEN questions w/ the user (batched, concise) whilst the run proceeds. FORMAT (user 202607131409): each question is its OWN numbered bullet, never a plain - bullet and never grouped/paraphrased away — - Q01. xxx..., - Q02. xxx... etc. (sequential across the whole batch, not per-cluster); thematic grouping/summarising is fine ABOVE the list as orientation, but every individual OPEN question still gets its own Q[nn] line so the user can answer by number. Do NOT re-raise during the run (the user is away; it wastes tokens): when a NEW question lands mid-run (visible as a playbook_question event in status.jsonl), emit only ❓ New question added. in chat —— raise it in full ONLY if the user asks (e.g. "raise it", "any questions?"); if raised but unanswered, the user got busy —— leave questions as they are. If answered, move the verdict into AJAP_protocols/playbook.md § FACTS and DELETE the question line (growth audit 202607072041: ticked-and-kept lines made questions.md grow forever); unanswered ones simply resurface next #seek.
  • Preflight/doctor: ./.venv/bin/python -m AJAP_code.main --preflight (checks the SEEK-login and LLM-token gates; see AJAP_protocols/USER_MANUAL.md).
  • KILL SWITCH (user-blessed 202607070504): write PAUSE (idle after current card, browser stays) or STOP (graceful end) to AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— the ONLY sanctioned ways to halt the programme; never kill the process.
  • CONTROL CENTRE RESET (user-blessed 202607130451; every time the user prompts #seek, BEFORE launching): write all 4 anchors fresh — run-state → RUN (else the launch refuses instantly on a leftover STOP); appearance → DARK/LIGHT per the current clock rule (0100→dark, 0800→light) or the live macOS scheme, whichever the appearance module would pick right now; Qi → the FIRST entry of AJAP_protocols/queue.md's ## order: line (belt-and-braces — the code's own bootstrap already resets a stale Qi to the order head at every fresh start, so this is immediate visible correctness, not closing a real gap); wkstop → 95 (the user's own value from a PRIOR session must NOT survive into a new #seek — only a MISSING/malformed anchor self-seeds to 95 today; an existing stale value does not). After this reset, wkstop is the user's to edit for the REST of that session (e.g. mid-run, to raise/lower the buffer) — never silently overwrite it again once #seek has fired. #psl/#ccl disregard CONTROL.md entirely (structural — psl_mode never constructs the usage sampler), so this reset is not needed before those.
  • AR homes (since 202607071013; delete this bullet once the user confirms the gcl/ccl migration —— DevPlan P7.7 has the steps): NEW ARs are written to AJAP_repo/gcl/ by DEFAULT —— no env var needed, do NOT set AJAP_GCL_ROOT; old-AR dedupe is a separate hardcoded read-only scan of seek/gcl (plus 341 ledger-baked legacy keys), so the user's pending move can never cause re-applications.
  • Status (user asks "how's it going"): read the last lines of progress.log + usage.jsonl totals; report the 🎯 count in ONE short line.
  • Steer: write PAUSE (finishes current card then idles) / RUN / STOP.

Open walls (incl. the Google captcha) are shown in a read-only flag window on Display 2 ("PHL 273V5 (2)"), where they await the USER's manual solve — AJAP never auto-solves them.
- Mirror report (once per `#seek`, after starting AJAP and BEFORE raising questions): if a fresh `AJAP_code/runtime/mirror_report_*.md` exists, skim it and mention its one-line gist to the user; it lists AR files the ledger did not know (manual adds / #psl moves / migration), already self-healed.
- Playbook questions —— ONCE per runtime, right after the run starts: read `AJAP_protocols/questions.md`; raise all OPEN questions w/ the user (batched, concise) whilst the run proceeds. FORMAT (user 202607131409): each question is its OWN numbered bullet, never a plain `-` bullet and never grouped/paraphrased away — `- Q01. xxx...`, `- Q02. xxx...` etc. (sequential across the whole batch, not per-cluster); thematic grouping/summarising is fine ABOVE the list as orientation, but every individual OPEN question still gets its own `Q[nn]` line so the user can answer by number. Do NOT re-raise during the run (the user is away; it wastes tokens): when a NEW question lands mid-run (visible as a `playbook_question` event in status.jsonl), emit only `❓ New question added.` in chat —— raise it in full ONLY if the user asks (e.g. "raise it", "any questions?"); if raised but unanswered, the user got busy —— leave questions as they are. If answered, move the verdict into `AJAP_protocols/playbook.md § FACTS` and DELETE the question line (growth audit 202607072041: ticked-and-kept lines made questions.md grow forever); unanswered ones simply resurface next `#seek`.
- Preflight/doctor: `./.venv/bin/python -m AJAP_code.main --preflight` (checks the SEEK-login and LLM-token gates; see `AJAP_protocols/USER_MANUAL.md`).
- KILL SWITCH (user-blessed 202607070504): write `PAUSE` (idle after current card, browser stays) or `STOP` (graceful end) to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— the ONLY sanctioned ways to halt the programme; never kill the process.
- CONTROL CENTRE RESET (user-blessed 202607130451; every time the user prompts `#seek`, BEFORE launching): write all 4 anchors fresh — run-state → `RUN` (else the launch refuses instantly on a leftover `STOP`); appearance → `DARK`/`LIGHT` per the current clock rule (0100→dark, 0800→light) or the live macOS scheme, whichever the appearance module would pick right now; Qi → the FIRST entry of `AJAP_protocols/queue.md`'s `## order:` line (belt-and-braces — the code's own bootstrap already resets a stale Qi to the order head at every fresh start, so this is immediate visible correctness, not closing a real gap); `wkstop` → `95` (the user's own value from a PRIOR session must NOT survive into a new `#seek` — only a MISSING/malformed anchor self-seeds to 95 today; an existing stale value does not). After this reset, `wkstop` is the user's to edit for the REST of that session (e.g. mid-run, to raise/lower the buffer) — never silently overwrite it again once `#seek` has fired. `#psl`/`#ccl` disregard CONTROL.md entirely (structural — `psl_mode` never constructs the usage sampler), so this reset is not needed before those.
- AR homes (since 202607071013; delete this bullet once the user confirms the gcl/ccl migration —— DevPlan P7.7 has the steps): NEW ARs are written to `AJAP_repo/gcl/` by DEFAULT —— no env var needed, do NOT set `AJAP_GCL_ROOT`; old-AR dedupe is a separate hardcoded read-only scan of `seek/gcl` (plus 341 ledger-baked legacy keys), so the user's pending move can never cause re-applications.
- Status (user asks "how's it going"): read the last lines of `progress.log` + `usage.jsonl` totals; report the 🎯 count in ONE short line.
- Steer: write `PAUSE` (finishes current card then idles) / `RUN` / `STOP`.
- `control: [content]` (non-case-sensitive) = the user's EXPLICIT lever phrase: match `[content]` against CONTROL.md's own option sets — the run-state words (RUN/PAUSE/STOP), the appearance words (DARK/LIGHT), or the `Qi[nn]` pattern — and write the matched anchor accordingly (upper-cased; Qi zero-padded to 2 digits; find the anchor by CONTENT, not by counting lines — see the Surfaces bullet above). Never hard-code examples: the option sets above ARE the validation. If `[content]` matches none of the three (e.g. prose, several words), do NOT write anything — tell the user it is not a CONTROL.md option and confirm his meaning first (it may have been a hasty message). A Qi edit made while the programme sleeps on a usage limit is honoured on resume; a leftover Qi from a previous programme run is reset to the order head at start.
- BARE VERBS: a one-word user message that is exactly `RUN` / `PAUSE` / `STOP` / `LIGHT` / `DARK` / or `Qi[nn]` (case-insensitive, no `control:` prefix needed) is the SAME lever phrase — validate it against the three option sets exactly as `control: [content]` does above and write the matched anchor (or, if it matches none, tell the user and confirm his meaning first). Keep the `control:` phrase too — this is an additional way in, not a replacement.
- CONFIRM DUTY: after writing an anchor (by either trigger above), OR after noticing the user hand-edited CONTROL.md himself, watch for the change to MANIFEST in reality — on each wake (roughly every 10 min is fine; no busy-polling) check the relevant signal: `STOP` → the process has exited or `status.jsonl`/`progress.log` shows the run ended; `PAUSE` → no further APPLIED/SKIP/VOID/PENDING lines land while the process stays alive; `RUN` → card processing is visibly proceeding again; `DARK`/`LIGHT` → CONTROL.md's own appearance anchor still reads the requested word after the next card's appearance-apply tick (i.e. it was not immediately re-mirrored back by a system/clock event); `Qi[nn]` → a `Qi[nn] ... p1: N cards` (or similar) progress line for that Qi has appeared. Once manifested, say so concisely and ONCE: `"[word] in effect"` (e.g. `"LIGHT in effect"`, `"Qi04 in effect"`) — nothing more. A plain-prose question from the user ("is Qi04 in effect now?") needs no trigger at all — just answer from the live state, same signals as above.
- Graceful stop (user says "stop"): write `STOP` (the programme finishes the in-flight card first —— tokens already invested); once the process exits, run `python3 "dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/gscpt/ajap_logs.py"` to produce the user's CSV (if it errors "CSV already exists", tell the user which file blocks it); then declare the runtime officially ended w/ the final 🎯 count. Under `#eng`, ALSO do eng.md's wrap-up.
- The program auto-resumes after session-limit stops; if the PROCESS itself is dead (crash, reboot), just relaunch —— the ledger resumes exactly where it stopped. `automator/Continue.app` is only a backstop for THIS cockpit chat.

## Rules

- NEVER summon user (interactive-question tool); whether or not user responds MUST NOT affect AJAP's fully autonomous running
- `dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/seek/` stays READ-ONLY except the program's own AR writes into `seek/gcl/` (create-only). Never edit protocol files there.
- NEVER touch `seek/interviews/` in cockpit mode (inherited hard rule from the old `seek/CLAUDE.md`; it stays in `seek/` until the `career/` migration).
- Never fabricate status —— everything you report must come from the runtime files. If they're silent, say the process appears stopped and offer relaunch.
- Chat discipline per root CLAUDE.md still applies to comms with the user.

AJAP Cockpit (live-session CLAUDE.md)

Read this ONLY when deliberately overseeing a live AJAP run. The system itself is code (AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/); you are its COCKPIT, not its engine. Do NOT re-enact the old CC-session AJAP (no heartbeats, no SA spawning, no mandatory 9-file reads —— those belong to the retired seek/ system).

Your Role

DEFINITIONS: the cockpit is the PLACE — this CC session, entered on #seek. The coordinator is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme, relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain language, and raise playbook questions. If the user prompts #seek #eng, you ALSO hold the engineer role — read AJAP_protocols/eng.md and follow it on top of everything here. The programme (AJAP_code/) is the engine; an #inv session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

Surfaces

  • AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— WRITE RUN / PAUSE / STOP on its run-state line (polled every card); this is your only lever, alongside the appearance (DARK/LIGHT), Qi (Qi[nn]), and weekly-quota-stop (wkstop, a bare 0-100 integer; 100 = never auto-stop) lines just below it. CONTENT-ANCHORED: every line is found by matching its own value, never by a fixed line number (see AJAP_code/core/control_io.py) — the shipped layout is heading, blank line, then the four anchor lines, then a ## subheading and explanatory prose, but a reordered or hand-edited file still resolves correctly as long as each anchor's line holds ONLY its bare word (RUN/PAUSE/STOP, DARK/LIGHT, Qi + digits, or a bare 0-100 integer) — never edit a line that also carries prose. wkstop is written by the usage sampler itself (never by you) when a scanned weekly-% reaches it — a run stopping with no STOP request from the user or coordinator may simply be this. (Moved out of runtime/ 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log —— READ for the human log (applied / skipped / voided, N counts).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl —— READ for structured events.
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/usage.jsonl —— READ for token usage + limit events (each limit event carries its parsed reset time).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md —— READ for the per-runtime decision trail (the new system's rlog_ equivalent) when debugging a run.

Operations

  • Start (on #seek or user asks) —— one quick foreground Bash: cd AJAP_repo && nohup caffeinate -ims ./.venv/bin/python -u -m AJAP_code.main > AJAP_code/runtime/nohup_[TS].log 2>&1 & disown —— flags: --test harmless clerk queue; --max N cap; --parallel N only after user approval. The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps running even if this chat is stopped; -u = unbuffered log; caffeinate -ims = no App Nap/disk-sleep.
  • If memory pressure is high before a long run (memory_pressure | tail -1), free RAM by closing Mail —— user-authorised (202607061831/202607061940; Mail never holds anything unsaved): PRIMARY osascript -e 'quit app "Mail"' (may fail on a permission dialog); FALLBACK if still running after ~10s: killall Mail, then killall -9 Mail —— a harmless kill that ensures the memory killer never has to pick the AJAP programme instead.
  • Google Drive: NEVER kill or force-quit it —— that is doomsday for the user (a past force-kill corrupted sync and cost a week's re-mirror). There is NO CLI graceful quit for it. Never ask the user mid-run either (AJAP is fully autonomous; a question stalls it) —— simply leave Drive alone, always; only the USER ever quits it (menu-bar icon → gear → Quit).
  • ACTIVE 🎯🚀 reporting (mandatory; format per user 202607080322 §277.8): after starting, spawn a background Monitor tailing AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log; on each new APPLIED/SKIP/VOID/ PENDING line, emit exactly this 3-line block in chat (nothing else):
  **[current_TS]**
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied.

[JP] and [JA] come from the log's (JA=…, JP=…) fields (carry the last seen [JA] forward on non-APPLIED lines), BOTH written as emoji numbers (e.g. 4️⃣2️⃣), never integers. This costs only a few hundred tokens per job. EXCEPTION LINES (the ONE canonical home for this doctrine; relay each critically in chat, verbatim quote + the fix): 🚨 GOOGLE CAPTCHA — research disabled Google for the runtime; only the user can clear the captcha at the machine (next start re-enables). 🚨 [HOST] WALL — that host raised a human-verification wall; the source is skipped this runtime, never auto-solved. ✅ [HOST] WALL healed — the user solved that wall (the flag window's re-probe noticed); the source is re-enabled this runtime. And 🚨 GLASSDOOR BLUR — Glassdoor re-gated content behind "share your experience" (returns roughly yearly); only the user can unlock it by sharing another experience on Glassdoor (alert-only — not a wall, no flag window). Open walls (incl. the Google captcha) are shown in a read-only flag window on Display 2 ("PHL 273V5 (2)"), where they await the USER's manual solve — AJAP never auto-solves them.

  • Mirror report (once per #seek, after starting AJAP and BEFORE raising questions): if a fresh AJAP_code/runtime/mirror_report_*.md exists, skim it and mention its one-line gist to the user; it lists AR files the ledger did not know (manual adds / #psl moves / migration), already self-healed.
  • Playbook questions —— ONCE per runtime, right after the run starts: read AJAP_protocols/questions.md; raise all OPEN questions w/ the user (batched, concise) whilst the run proceeds. - Status (user asks "how's it going"): read the last lines of `progress.log` + `usage.jsonl` totals; report the 🎯 count in ONE short line.
- Steer: write `PAUSE` (finishes current card then idles) / `RUN` / `STOP`.
- `control: [content]` (non-case-sensitive) = the user's EXPLICIT lever phrase: match `[content]` against CONTROL.md's own option sets — the run-state words (RUN/PAUSE/STOP), the appearance words (DARK/LIGHT), or the `Qi[nn]` pattern — and write the matched anchor accordingly (upper-cased; Qi zero-padded to 2 digits; find the anchor by CONTENT, not by counting lines — see the Surfaces bullet above). Never hard-code examples: the option sets above ARE the validation. If `[content]` matches none of the three (e.g. prose, several words), do NOT write anything — tell the user it is not a CONTROL.md option and confirm his meaning first (it may have been a hasty message). A Qi edit made while the programme sleeps on a usage limit is honoured on resume; a leftover Qi from a previous programme run is reset to the order head at start.
- BARE VERBS: a one-word user message that is exactly `RUN` / `PAUSE` / `STOP` / `LIGHT` / `DARK` / or `Qi[nn]` (case-insensitive, no `control:` prefix needed) is the SAME lever phrase — validate it against the three option sets exactly as `control: [content]` does above and write the matched anchor (or, if it matches none, tell the user and confirm his meaning first). Keep the `control:` phrase too — this is an additional way in, not a replacement.
- CONFIRM DUTY: after writing an anchor (by either trigger above), OR after noticing the user hand-edited CONTROL.md himself, watch for the change to MANIFEST in reality — on each wake (roughly every 10 min is fine; no busy-polling) check the relevant signal: `STOP` → the process has exited or `status.jsonl`/`progress.log` shows the run ended; `PAUSE` → no further APPLIED/SKIP/VOID/PENDING lines land while the process stays alive; `RUN` → card processing is visibly proceeding again; `DARK`/`LIGHT` → CONTROL.md's own appearance anchor still reads the requested word after the next card's appearance-apply tick (i.e. it was not immediately re-mirrored back by a system/clock event); `Qi[nn]` → a `Qi[nn] ... p1: N cards` (or similar) progress line for that Qi has appeared. Once manifested, say so concisely and ONCE: `"[word] in effect"` (e.g. `"LIGHT in effect"`, `"Qi04 in effect"`) — nothing more. A plain-prose question from the user ("is Qi04 in effect now?") needs no trigger at all — just answer from the live state, same signals as above.
- Graceful stop (user says "stop"): write `STOP` (the programme finishes the in-flight card first —— tokens already invested); once the process exits, run `python3 "dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/gscpt/ajap_logs.py"` to produce the user's CSV (if it errors "CSV already exists", tell the user which file blocks it); then declare the runtime officially ended w/ the final 🎯 count. Under `#eng`, ALSO do eng.md's wrap-up.
- The program auto-resumes after session-limit stops; if the PROCESS itself is dead (crash, reboot), just relaunch —— the ledger resumes exactly where it stopped. `automator/Continue.app` is only a backstop for THIS cockpit chat.

## Rules

- NEVER summon user (interactive-question tool); whether or not user responds MUST NOT affect AJAP's fully autonomous running
- `dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/seek/` stays READ-ONLY except the program's own AR writes into `seek/gcl/` (create-only). Never edit protocol files there.
- NEVER touch `seek/interviews/` in cockpit mode (inherited hard rule from the old `seek/CLAUDE.md`; it stays in `seek/` until the `career/` migration).
- Never fabricate status —— everything you report must come from the runtime files. If they're silent, say the process appears stopped and offer relaunch.
- Chat discipline per root CLAUDE.md still applies to comms with the user.

AJAP Cockpit (live-session CLAUDE.md)

Read this ONLY when deliberately overseeing a live AJAP run. The system itself is code (AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/); you are its COCKPIT, not its engine. Do NOT re-enact the old CC-session AJAP (no heartbeats, no SA spawning, no mandatory 9-file reads —— those belong to the retired seek/ system).

Your Role

DEFINITIONS: the cockpit is the PLACE — this CC session, entered on #seek. The coordinator is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme, relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain language, and raise playbook questions. If the user prompts #seek #eng, you ALSO hold the engineer role — read AJAP_protocols/eng.md and follow it on top of everything here. The programme (AJAP_code/) is the engine; an #inv session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

Surfaces

  • AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— WRITE RUN / PAUSE / STOP on its run-state line (polled every card); this is your only lever, alongside the appearance (DARK/LIGHT), Qi (Qi[nn]), and weekly-quota-stop (wkstop, a bare 0-100 integer; 100 = never auto-stop) lines just below it. CONTENT-ANCHORED: every line is found by matching its own value, never by a fixed line number (see AJAP_code/core/control_io.py) — the shipped layout is heading, blank line, then the four anchor lines, then a ## subheading and explanatory prose, but a reordered or hand-edited file still resolves correctly as long as each anchor's line holds ONLY its bare word (RUN/PAUSE/STOP, DARK/LIGHT, Qi + digits, or a bare 0-100 integer) — never edit a line that also carries prose. wkstop is written by the usage sampler itself (never by you) when a scanned weekly-% reaches it — a run stopping with no STOP request from the user or coordinator may simply be this. (Moved out of runtime/ 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log —— READ for the human log (applied / skipped / voided, N counts).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl —— READ for structured events.
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/usage.jsonl —— READ for token usage + limit events (each limit event carries its parsed reset time).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md —— READ for the per-runtime decision trail (the new system's rlog_ equivalent) when debugging a run.

Operations

  • Start (on #seek or user asks) —— one quick foreground Bash: cd AJAP_repo && nohup caffeinate -ims ./.venv/bin/python -u -m AJAP_code.main > AJAP_code/runtime/nohup_[TS].log 2>&1 & disown —— flags: --test harmless clerk queue; --max N cap; --parallel N only after user approval. The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps running even if this chat is stopped; -u = unbuffered log; caffeinate -ims = no App Nap/disk-sleep.
  • If memory pressure is high before a long run (memory_pressure | tail -1), free RAM by closing Mail —— user-authorised (202607061831/202607061940; Mail never holds anything unsaved): PRIMARY osascript -e 'quit app "Mail"' (may fail on a permission dialog); FALLBACK if still running after ~10s: killall Mail, then killall -9 Mail —— a harmless kill that ensures the memory killer never has to pick the AJAP programme instead.
  • Google Drive: NEVER kill or force-quit it —— that is doomsday for the user (a past force-kill corrupted sync and cost a week's re-mirror). There is NO CLI graceful quit for it. Never ask the user mid-run either (AJAP is fully autonomous; a question stalls it) —— simply leave Drive alone, always; only the USER ever quits it (menu-bar icon → gear → Quit).
  • ACTIVE 🎯🚀 reporting (mandatory; format per user 202607080322 §277.8): after starting, spawn a background Monitor tailing AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log; on each new APPLIED/SKIP/VOID/ PENDING line, emit exactly this 3-line block in chat (nothing else):
  **[current_TS]**
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied.

[JP] and [JA] come from the log's (JA=…, JP=…) fields (carry the last seen [JA] forward on non-APPLIED lines), BOTH written as emoji numbers (e.g. 4️⃣2️⃣), never integers. This costs only a few hundred tokens per job. EXCEPTION LINES (the ONE canonical home for this doctrine; relay each critically in chat, verbatim quote + the fix): 🚨 GOOGLE CAPTCHA — research disabled Google for the runtime; only the user can clear the captcha at the machine (next start re-enables). 🚨 [HOST] WALL — that host raised a human-verification wall; the source is skipped this runtime, never auto-solved. ✅ [HOST] WALL healed — the user solved that wall (the flag window's re-probe noticed); the source is re-enabled this runtime. And 🚨 GLASSDOOR BLUR — Glassdoor re-gated content behind "share your experience" (returns roughly yearly); only the user can unlock it by sharing another experience on Glassdoor (alert-only — not a wall, no flag window). Open walls (incl. the Google captcha) are shown in a read-only flag window on Display 2 ("PHL 273V5 (2)"), where they await the USER's manual solve — AJAP never auto-solves them.

  • Mirror report (once per #seek, after starting AJAP and BEFORE raising questions): if a fresh AJAP_code/runtime/mirror_report_*.md exists, skim it and mention its one-line gist to the user; it lists AR files the ledger did not know (manual adds / #psl moves / migration), already self-healed.
  • Playbook questions —— ONCE per runtime, right after the run starts: read AJAP_protocols/questions.md; raise all OPEN questions w/ the user (batched, concise) whilst the run proceeds. Do NOT re-raise during the run (the user is away; it wastes tokens): when a NEW question lands mid-run (visible as a playbook_question event in status.jsonl), emit only ❓ New question added. in chat —— raise it in full ONLY if the user asks (e.g. "raise it", "any questions?"); if raised but unanswered, the user got busy —— leave questions as they are. If answered, move the verdict into AJAP_protocols/playbook.md § FACTS and DELETE the question line (growth audit 202607072041: ticked-and-kept lines made questions.md grow forever); unanswered ones simply resurface next #seek.
  • Preflight/doctor: ./.venv/bin/python -m AJAP_code.main --preflight (checks the SEEK-login and LLM-token gates; see AJAP_protocols/USER_MANUAL.md).
  • KILL SWITCH (user-blessed 202607070504): write PAUSE (idle after current card, browser stays) or STOP (graceful end) to AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— the ONLY sanctioned ways to halt the programme; never kill the process.
  • CONTROL CENTRE RESET (user-blessed 202607130451; every time the user prompts #seek, BEFORE launching): write all 4 anchors fresh — run-state → RUN (else the launch refuses instantly on a leftover STOP); appearance → DARK/LIGHT per the current clock rule (0100→dark, 0800→light) or the live macOS scheme, whichever the appearance module would pick right now; Qi → the FIRST entry of AJAP_protocols/queue.md's ## order: line (belt-and-braces — the code's own bootstrap already resets a stale Qi to the order head at every fresh start, so this is immediate visible correctness, not closing a real gap); wkstop → 95 (the user's own value from a PRIOR session must NOT survive into a new #seek — only a MISSING/malformed anchor self-seeds to 95 today; an existing stale value does not).   • AR homes (since 202607071013; delete this bullet once the user confirms the gcl/ccl migration —— DevPlan P7.7 has the steps): NEW ARs are written to AJAP_repo/gcl/ by DEFAULT —— no env var needed, do NOT set AJAP_GCL_ROOT; old-AR dedupe is a separate hardcoded read-only scan of seek/gcl (plus 341 ledger-baked legacy keys), so the user's pending move can never cause re-applications.

The coordinator is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme, relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain language, and raise playbook questions. If the user prompts #seek #eng, you ALSO hold the engineer role — read AJAP_protocols/eng.md and follow it on top of everything here. The programme (AJAP_code/) is the engine; an #inv session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

Surfaces

  • AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— WRITE RUN / PAUSE / STOP on its run-state line (polled every card); this is your only lever, alongside the appearance (DARK/LIGHT), Qi (Qi[nn]), and weekly-quota-stop (wkstop, a bare 0-100 integer; 100 = never auto-stop) lines just below it. CONTENT-ANCHORED: every line is found by matching its own value, never by a fixed line number (see AJAP_code/core/control_io.py) — the shipped layout is heading, blank line, then the four anchor lines, then a ## subheading and explanatory prose, but a reordered or hand-edited file still resolves correctly as long as each anchor's line holds ONLY its bare word (RUN/PAUSE/STOP, DARK/LIGHT, Qi + digits, or a bare 0-100 integer) — never edit a line that also carries prose. wkstop is written by the usage sampler itself (never by you) when a scanned weekly-% reaches it — a run stopping with no STOP request from the user or coordinator may simply be this. (Moved out of runtime/ 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log —— READ for the human log (applied / skipped / voided, N counts).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl —— READ for structured events.
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/usage.jsonl —— READ for token usage + limit events (each limit event carries its parsed reset time).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md —— READ for the per-runtime decision trail (the new system's rlog_ equivalent) when debugging a run.

Operations

  • Start (on #seek or user asks) —— one quick foreground Bash: cd AJAP_repo && nohup caffeinate -ims ./.venv/bin/python -u -m AJAP_code.main > AJAP_code/runtime/nohup_[TS].log 2>&1 & disown —— flags: --test harmless clerk queue; --max N cap; --parallel N only after user approval. The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps running even if this chat is stopped; -u = unbuffered log; caffeinate -ims = no App Nap/disk-sleep.
  • If memory pressure is high before a long run (memory_pressure | tail -1), free RAM by closing Mail —— user-authorised (202607061831/202607061940; Mail never holds anything unsaved): PRIMARY osascript -e 'quit app "Mail"' (may fail on a permission dialog); FALLBACK if still running after ~10s: killall Mail, then killall -9 Mail —— a harmless kill that ensures the memory killer never has to pick the AJAP programme instead.
  • Google Drive: NEVER kill or force-quit it —— that is doomsday for the user (a past force-kill corrupted sync and cost a week's re-mirror). There is NO CLI graceful quit for it. Never ask the user mid-run either (AJAP is fully autonomous; a question stalls it) —— simply leave Drive alone, always; only the USER ever quits it (menu-bar icon → gear → Quit).
  • ACTIVE 🎯🚀 reporting (mandatory; format per user 202607080322 §277.8): after starting, spawn a background Monitor tailing AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log; on each new APPLIED/SKIP/VOID/ PENDING line, emit exactly this 3-line block in chat (nothing else):
  **[current_TS]**
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied.

[JP] and [JA] come from the log's (JA=…, JP=…) fields (carry the last seen [JA] forward on non-APPLIED lines), BOTH written as emoji numbers (e.g. 4️⃣2️⃣), never integers. This costs only a few hundred tokens per job. EXCEPTION LINES (the ONE canonical home for this doctrine; relay each critically in chat, verbatim quote + the fix): 🚨 GOOGLE CAPTCHA — research disabled Google for the runtime; only the user can clear the captcha at the machine (next start re-enables). 🚨 [HOST] WALL — that host raised a human-verification wall; the source is skipped this runtime, never auto-solved. ✅ [HOST] WALL healed — the user solved that wall (the flag window's re-probe noticed); the source is re-enabled this runtime. And 🚨 GLASSDOOR BLUR — Glassdoor re-gated content behind "share your experience" (returns roughly yearly); only the user can unlock it by sharing another experience on Glassdoor (alert-only — not a wall, no flag window). Open walls (incl. the Google captcha) are shown in a read-only flag window on Display 2 ("PHL 273V5 (2)"), where they await the USER's manual solve — AJAP never auto-solves them.

  • Mirror report (once per #seek, after starting AJAP and BEFORE raising questions): if a fresh AJAP_code/runtime/mirror_report_*.md exists, skim it and mention its one-line gist to the user; it lists AR files the ledger did not know (manual adds / #psl moves / migration), already self-healed.
  • Playbook questions —— ONCE per runtime, right after the run starts: read AJAP_protocols/questions.md; raise all OPEN questions w/ the user (batched, concise) whilst the run proceeds. Do NOT re-raise during the run (the user is away; it wastes tokens): when a NEW question lands mid-run (visible as a playbook_question event in status.jsonl), emit only ❓ New question added. in chat —— raise it in full ONLY if the user asks (e.g. "raise it", "any questions?"); if raised but unanswered, the user got busy —— leave questions as they are. If answered, move the verdict into AJAP_protocols/playbook.md § FACTS and DELETE the question line (growth audit 202607072041: ticked-and-kept lines made questions.md grow forever); unanswered ones simply resurface next #seek.
  • Preflight/doctor: ./.venv/bin/python -m AJAP_code.main --preflight (checks the SEEK-login and LLM-token gates; see AJAP_protocols/USER_MANUAL.md).
  • KILL SWITCH (user-blessed 202607070504): write PAUSE (idle after current card, browser stays) or STOP (graceful end) to AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— the ONLY sanctioned ways to halt the programme; never kill the process.
  • CONTROL CENTRE RESET (user-blessed 202607130451; every time the user prompts #seek, BEFORE launching): write all 4 anchors fresh — run-state → RUN (else the launch refuses instantly on a leftover STOP); appearance → DARK/LIGHT per the current clock rule (0100→dark, 0800→light) or the live macOS scheme, whichever the appearance module would pick right now; Qi → the FIRST entry of AJAP_protocols/queue.md's ## order: line (belt-and-braces — the code's own bootstrap already resets a stale Qi to the order head at every fresh start, so this is immediate visible correctness, not closing a real gap); wkstop → 95 (the user's own value from a PRIOR session must NOT survive into a new #seek — only a MISSING/malformed anchor self-seeds to 95 today; an existing stale value does not). After this reset, wkstop is the user's to edit for the REST of that session (e.g. mid-run, to raise/lower the buffer) — never silently overwrite it again once #seek has fired. #psl/#ccl disregard CONTROL.md entirely (structural — psl_mode never constructs the usage sampler), so this reset is not needed before those.
  • AR homes (since 202607071013; delete this bullet once the user confirms the gcl/ccl migration —— DevPlan P7.7 has the steps): NEW ARs are written to AJAP_repo/gcl/ by DEFAULT —— no env var needed, do NOT set AJAP_GCL_ROOT; old-AR dedupe is a separate hardcoded read-only scan of seek/gcl (plus 341 ledger-baked legacy keys), so the user's pending move can never cause re-applications.
  • Status (user asks "how's it going"): read the last lines of progress.log + usage.jsonl totals; report the 🎯 count in ONE short line.
  • Steer: write PAUSE (finishes current card then idles) / RUN / STOP.
  • control: [content] (non-case-sensitive) = the user's EXPLICIT lever phrase: match [content] against CONTROL.md's own option sets — the run-state words (RUN/PAUSE/STOP), the appearance words (DARK/LIGHT), or the Qi[nn] pattern — and write the matched anchor accordingly (upper-cased; Qi zero-padded to 2 digits; find the anchor by CONTENT, not by counting lines — see the Surfaces bullet above). Never hard-code examples: the option sets above ARE the validation. If [content] matches none of the three (e.g. prose, several words), do NOT write anything — tell the user it is not a CONTROL.md option and confirm his meaning first (it may have been a hasty message). A Qi edit made while the programme sleeps on a usage limit is honoured on resume; a leftover Qi from a previous programme run is reset to the order head at start.
  • BARE VERBS: a one-word user message that is exactly RUN / PAUSE / STOP / LIGHT / DARK / or Qi[nn] (case-insensitive, no control: prefix needed) is the SAME lever phrase — validate it against the three option sets exactly as control: [content] does above and write the matched anchor (or, if it matches none, tell the user and confirm his meaning first). Keep the control: phrase too — this is an additional way in, not a replacement.
  • CONFIRM DUTY: after writing an anchor (by either trigger above), OR after noticing the user hand-edited CONTROL.md himself, watch for the change to MANIFEST in reality — on each wake (roughly every 10 min is fine; no busy-polling) check the relevant signal: STOP → the process has exited or status.jsonl/progress.log shows the run ended; PAUSE → no further APPLIED/SKIP/VOID/PENDING lines land while the process stays alive; RUN → card processing is visibly proceeding again; DARK/LIGHT → CONTROL.md's own appearance anchor still reads the requested word after the next card's appearance-apply tick (i.e. it was not immediately re-mirrored back by a system/clock event); Qi[nn] → a Qi[nn] ... p1: N cards (or similar) progress line for that Qi has appeared. Once manifested, say so concisely and ONCE: "[word] in effect" (e.g. "LIGHT in effect", "Qi04 in effect") — nothing more. A plain-prose question from the user ("is Qi04 in effect now?") needs no trigger at all — just answer from the live state, same signals as above.
  • Graceful stop (user says "stop"): write STOP (the programme finishes the in-flight card first —— tokens already invested); once the process exits, run python3 "dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/gscpt/ajap_logs.py" to produce the user's CSV (if it errors "CSV already exists", tell the user which file blocks it); then declare the runtime officially ended w/ the final 🎯 count. Under #eng, ALSO do eng.md's wrap-up.
  • The program auto-resumes after session-limit stops; if the PROCESS itself is dead (crash, reboot), just relaunch —— the ledger resumes exactly where it stopped. automator/Continue.app is only a backstop for THIS cockpit chat.

Rules

  • dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/seek/ stays READ-ONLY except the program's own AR writes into seek/gcl/ (create-only). Never edit protocol files there.
  • NEVER touch seek/interviews/ in cockpit mode (inherited hard rule from the old seek/CLAUDE.md; it stays in seek/ until the career/ migration).
  • Never fabricate status —— everything you report must come from the runtime files. If they're silent, say the process appears stopped and offer relaunch.
  • Chat discipline per root CLAUDE.md still applies to comms with the user.

# AJAP Cockpit (live-session CLAUDE.md)

*Read this ONLY when deliberately overseeing a live AJAP run. The system itself is code (`AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/`); you are its COCKPIT, not its engine. Do NOT re-enact the old CC-session AJAP (no heartbeats, no SA spawning, no mandatory 9-file reads —— those belong to the retired `seek/` system).*

## Your Role

DEFINITIONS: the **cockpit** is the PLACE — this CC session, entered on `#seek`. The **coordinator** is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme, relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain language, and raise playbook questions. If the user prompts `#seek #eng`, you ALSO hold the **engineer** role — read `AJAP_protocols/eng.md` and follow it on top of everything here. The programme (`AJAP_code/`) is the engine; an `#inv` session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

## Surfaces

- `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— WRITE `RUN` / `PAUSE` / `STOP` on its run-state line (polled every card).card); Thisthis is your only lever;lever, onealongside the appearance (`DARK`/`LIGHT`) and Qi (`Qi[nn]`) lines just below it. (Moved out of `runtime/` 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log` —— READ for the human log (applied / skipped / voided, N counts).
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl` —— READ for structured events.

The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps running even if this chat is stopped; `-u` = unbuffered log; `caffeinate -ims` = no App Nap/disk-sleep.

  ```

This costs only a few hundred tokens per job.   • KILL SWITCH (user-blessed 202607070504): write PAUSE (idle after current card, browser stays) or STOP (graceful end) to AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— the ONLY sanctioned ways to halt the programme; never kill the process.
  • AR homes (since 202607071013; delete this bullet once the user confirms the gcl/ccl migration —— DevPlan P7.7 has the steps): NEW ARs are written to AJAP_repo/gcl/ by DEFAULT —— no env var needed, do NOT set AJAP_GCL_ROOT; old-AR dedupe is a separate hardcoded read-only scan of seek/gcl (plus 341 ledger-baked legacy keys), so the user's pending move can never cause re-applications.
  • Status (user asks "how's it going"): read the last lines of progress.log + usage.jsonl totals; report the 🎯 count in ONE short line.
  • Steer: write PAUSE (finishes current card then idles) / RUN / STOP.
  • control: [content] (non-case-sensitive) = the user's EXPLICIT lever phrase: match [content] against CONTROL.md's own option sets — the run-state words (RUN/PAUSE/STOP), the appearance words (DARK/LIGHT), or the Qi[nn] pattern — and write the matched anchor accordingly (upper-cased; Qi zero-padded to 2 digits; find the anchor by CONTENT, not by counting lines — see the Surfaces bullet above). Never hard-code examples: the option sets above ARE the validation. If [content] matches none of the three (e.g. prose, several words), do NOT write anything — tell the user it is not a CONTROL.md option and confirm his meaning first (it may have been a hasty message). A Qi edit made while the programme sleeps on a usage limit is honoured on resume; a leftover Qi from a previous programme run is reset to the order head at start.
  • BARE VERBS: a one-word user message that is exactly RUN / PAUSE / STOP / LIGHT / DARK / or Qi[nn] (case-insensitive, no control: prefix needed) is the SAME lever phrase — validate it against the three option sets exactly as control: [content] does above and write the matched anchor (or, if it matches none, tell the user and confirm his meaning first). Keep the control: phrase too — this is an additional way in, not a replacement.
  • CONFIRM DUTY: after writing an anchor (by either trigger above), OR after noticing the user hand-edited CONTROL.md himself, watch for the change to MANIFEST in reality — on each wake (roughly every 10 min is fine; no busy-polling) check the relevant signal: STOP → the process has exited or status.jsonl/progress.log shows the run ended; PAUSE → no further APPLIED/SKIP/VOID/PENDING lines land while the process stays alive; RUN → card processing is visibly proceeding again; DARK/LIGHT → CONTROL.md's own appearance anchor still reads the requested word after the next card's appearance-apply tick (i.e. it was not immediately re-mirrored back by a system/clock event); Qi[nn] → a Qi[nn] ... p1: N cards (or similar) progress line for that Qi has appeared. Once manifested, say so concisely and ONCE: "[word] in effect" (e.g. "LIGHT in effect", "Qi04 in effect") — nothing more. A plain-prose question from the user ("is Qi04 in effect now?") needs no trigger at all — just answer from the live state, same signals as above.
  • Graceful stop (user says "stop"): write STOP (the programme finishes the in-flight card first —— tokens already invested); once the process exits, run python3 "dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/gscpt/ajap_logs.py" to produce the user's CSV (if it errors "CSV already exists", tell the user which file blocks it); then declare the runtime officially ended w/ the final 🎯 count. Under #eng, ALSO do eng.md's wrap-up.
  • The program auto-resumes after session-limit stops; if the PROCESS itself is dead (crash, reboot), just relaunch —— the ledger resumes exactly where it stopped. automator/Continue.app is only a backstop for THIS cockpit chat.

Rules

  • dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/seek/ stays READ-ONLY except the program's own AR writes into seek/gcl/ (create-only). Never edit protocol files there.
  • NEVER touch seek/interviews/ in cockpit mode (inherited hard rule from the old seek/CLAUDE.md; it stays in seek/ until the career/ migration).
  • Never fabricate status —— everything you report must come from the runtime files. If they're silent, say the process appears stopped and offer relaunch.
  • Chat discipline per root CLAUDE.md still applies to comms with the user.

# AJAP Cockpit (live-session CLAUDE.md)

*Read this ONLY when deliberately overseeing a live AJAP run. The system itself is code (`AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/`); you are its COCKPIT, not its engine. Do NOT re-enact the old CC-session AJAP (no heartbeats, no SA spawning, no mandatory 9-file reads —— those belong to the retired `seek/` system).*

## Your Role

DEFINITIONS: the **cockpit** is the PLACE — this CC session, entered on `#seek`. The **coordinator** is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme, relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain language, and raise playbook questions. If the user prompts `#seek #eng`, you ALSO hold the **engineer** role — read `AJAP_protocols/eng.md` and follow it on top of everything here. The programme (`AJAP_code/`) is the engine; an `#inv` session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

## Surfaces

- `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— WRITE `RUN` / `PAUSE` / `STOP` (polled every card). This is your only lever; one word on line 1. (Moved out of `runtime/` 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log` —— READ for the human log (applied / skipped / voided, N counts).
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl` —— READ for structured events.
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/usage.jsonl` —— READ for token usage + limit events (each limit event carries its parsed reset time).
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md` —— READ for the per-runtime decision trail (the new system's `rlog_` equivalent) when debugging a run.

## Operations

- Start (on `#seek` or user asks) —— one quick foreground Bash: `cd AJAP_repo && nohup caffeinate -ims ./.venv/bin/python -u -m AJAP_code.main > AJAP_code/runtime/nohup_[TS].log 2>&1 & disown` —— flags: `--test` harmless clerk queue; `--max N` cap; `--parallel N` only after user approval. The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps running even if this chat is stopped; `-u` = unbuffered log; `caffeinate -ims` = no App Nap/disk-sleep.
- If memory pressure is high before a long run (`memory_pressure | tail -1`), free RAM by closing Mail —— user-authorised (202607061831/202607061940; Mail never holds anything unsaved): PRIMARY `osascript -e 'quit app "Mail"'` (may fail on a permission dialog); FALLBACK if still running after `~`10s: `killall Mail`, then `killall -9 Mail` —— a harmless kill that ensures the memory killer never has to pick the AJAP programme instead.
- Google Drive: NEVER kill or force-quit it —— that is doomsday for the user (a past force-kill corrupted sync and cost a week's re-mirror). There is NO CLI graceful quit for it. Never ask the user mid-run either (AJAP is fully autonomous; a question stalls it) —— simply leave Drive alone, always; only the USER ever quits it (menu-bar icon → gear → Quit).
- ACTIVE 🎯🚀 reporting (mandatory; format per user 202607080322 §277.8): after starting, spawn a background Monitor tailing `AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log`; on each new `APPLIED`/`SKIP`/`VOID`/ `PENDING` line, emit exactly this 3-line block in chat (nothing else):

  ```
  **[current_TS]**
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied.
  ```

  [JP] and [JA] come from the log's `(JA=…, JP=…)` fields (carry the last seen [JA] forward on non-APPLIED lines), BOTH written as emoji numbers (e.g. 4️⃣2️⃣), never integers. This costs only a few hundred tokens per job. EXCEPTION LINES (relay(the ONE canonical home for this doctrine; relay each critically in chat, verbatim quote + the fix): `🚨 GOOGLE CAPTCHA` — research disabled Google for the runtime; only the user can clear the captcha at the machine (next start re-enables). `🚨 [HOST] WALL` — that host raised a human-verification wall; the source is skipped this runtime, never auto-solved. `✅ [HOST] WALL healed` — the user solved that wall (the flag window's re-probe noticed); the source is re-enabled this runtime. And `🚨 GLASSDOOR BLUR` — Glassdoor re-gated content behind "share your experience" (returns roughly yearly); only the user can unlock it by sharing another experience on Glassdoor.Glassdoor (alert-only — not a wall, no flag window). Open walls (incl. the Google captcha) are shown in a read-only flag window on Display 2 ("PHL 273V5 (2)"), where they await the USER's manual solve — AJAP never auto-solves them.
- Mirror report (once per `#seek`, after starting AJAP and BEFORE raising questions): if a fresh `AJAP_code/runtime/mirror_report_*.md` exists, skim it and mention its one-line gist to the user; it lists AR files the ledger did not know (manual adds / #psl moves / migration), already self-healed.
- Playbook questions —— ONCE per runtime, right after the run starts: read `AJAP_protocols/questions.md`; raise all OPEN questions w/ the user (batched, concise) whilst the run proceeds. Do NOT re-raise during the run (the user is away; it wastes tokens): when a NEW question lands mid-run (visible as a `playbook_question` event in status.jsonl), emit only `❓ New question added.` in chat —— raise it in full ONLY if the user asks (e.g. "raise it", "any questions?"); if raised but unanswered, the user got busy —— leave questions as they are. If answered, move the verdict into `AJAP_protocols/playbook.md § FACTS` and DELETE the question line (growth audit 202607072041: ticked-and-kept lines made questions.md grow forever); unanswered ones simply resurface next `#seek`.
- Preflight/doctor: `./.venv/bin/python -m AJAP_code.main --preflight` (checks the SEEK-login and LLM-token gates; see `AJAP_protocols/USER_MANUAL.md`).
- KILL SWITCH (user-blessed 202607070504): write `PAUSE` (idle after current card, browser stays) or `STOP` (graceful end) to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— the ONLY sanctioned ways to halt the programme; never kill the process.
- AR homes (since 202607071013; delete this bullet once the user confirms the gcl/ccl migration —— DevPlan P7.7 has the steps): NEW ARs are written to `AJAP_repo/gcl/` by DEFAULT —— no env var needed, do NOT set `AJAP_GCL_ROOT`; old-AR dedupe is a separate hardcoded read-only scan of `seek/gcl` (plus 341 ledger-baked legacy keys), so the user's pending move can never cause re-applications.
- Status (user asks "how's it going"): read the last lines of `progress.log` + `usage.jsonl` totals; report the 🎯 count in ONE short line.
- Steer: write `PAUSE` (finishes current card then idles) / `RUN` / `STOP`.
- `control: [content]` (non-case-sensitive) = the user's EXPLICIT lever phrase: match `[content]` against CONTROL.md's own option sets — the Line-1 words (RUN/PAUSE/STOP), the Line-2 words (DARK/LIGHT), or the Line-3 `Qi[nn]` pattern — and write the matched line accordingly (upper-cased; Qi zero-padded to 2 digits). Never hard-code examples: the option sets above ARE the validation. If `[content]` matches none of the three (e.g. prose, several words), do NOT write anything — tell the user it is not a CONTROL.md option and confirm his meaning first (it may have been a hasty message). A Line-3 edit made while the programme sleeps on a usage limit is honoured on resume; a leftover Qi from a previous programme run is reset to the order head at start.
- Graceful stop (user says "stop"): write `STOP` (the programme finishes the in-flight card first —— tokens already invested); once the process exits, run `python3 "dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/gscpt/ajap_logs.py"` to produce the user's CSV (if it errors "CSV already exists", tell the user which file blocks it); then declare the runtime officially ended w/ the final 🎯 count. Under `#eng`, ALSO do eng.md's wrap-up.
- The program auto-resumes after session-limit stops; if the PROCESS itself is dead (crash, reboot), just relaunch —— the ledger resumes exactly where it stopped. `automator/Continue.app` is only a backstop for THIS cockpit chat.

## Rules

- `dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/seek/` stays READ-ONLY except the program's own AR writes into `seek/gcl/` (create-only). Never edit protocol files there.
- NEVER touch `seek/interviews/` in cockpit mode (inherited hard rule from the old `seek/CLAUDE.md`; it stays in `seek/` until the `career/` migration).
- Never fabricate status —— everything you report must come from the runtime files. If they're silent, say the process appears stopped and offer relaunch.
- Chat discipline per root CLAUDE.md still applies to comms with the user.

   `AJAP_repo/gcl/` by DEFAULT —— no env var needed, do NOT set `AJAP_GCL_ROOT`;
   old-AR dedupe is a separate hardcoded read-only scan of `seek/gcl` (plus 341
   ledger-baked legacy keys), so the user's pending move can never cause
   re-applications.
- Status (user asks "how's it going"): read the last lines of `progress.log` +
   `usage.jsonl` totals; report the 🎯 count in ONE short line.
- Steer: write `PAUSE` (finishes current card then idles) / `RUN` / `STOP`.
- `control: [content]` (non-case-sensitive) = the user's EXPLICIT lever
   phrase: match `[content]` against CONTROL.md's own option sets — the
   Line-1 words (RUN/PAUSE/STOP), the Line-2 words (DARK/LIGHT), or the
   Line-3 `Qi[nn]` pattern — and write the matched line accordingly
   (upper-cased; Qi zero-padded to 2 digits). Never hard-code examples: the
   option sets above ARE the validation. If `[content]` matches none of the
   three (e.g. prose, several words), do NOT write anything — tell the user
   it is not a CONTROL.md option and confirm his meaning first (it may have
   been a hasty message).
- Graceful stop (user says "stop"): write `STOP` (the programme finishes the
   in-flight card first —— tokens already invested); once the process exits, run
   `python3 "dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/gscpt/ajap_logs.py"` to produce the user's CSV
   (if it errors "CSV already exists", tell the user which file blocks it); then
   declare the runtime officially ended w/ the final 🎯 count. Under `#eng`,
   ALSO do eng.md's wrap-up.
- The program auto-resumes after session-limit stops; if the PROCESS itself is
   dead (crash, reboot), just relaunch —— the ledger resumes exactly where it
   stopped. `automator/Continue.app` is only a backstop for THIS cockpit chat.

## Rules

- `dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/seek/` stays READ-ONLY except the program's own AR
   writes into `seek/gcl/` (create-only). Never edit protocol files there.
- NEVER touch `seek/interviews/` in cockpit mode (inherited hard rule from the
   old `seek/CLAUDE.md`; it stays in `seek/` until the `career/` migration).
- Never fabricate status —— everything you report must come from the runtime
   files. If they're silent, say the process appears stopped and offer relaunch.
- Chat discipline per root CLAUDE.md still applies to comms with the user.

AJAP Cockpit (live-session CLAUDE.md)

Read this ONLY when deliberately overseeing a live AJAP run. The system itself is code (AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/); you are its COCKPIT, not its engine. Do NOT re-enact the old CC-session AJAP (no heartbeats, no SA spawning, no mandatory 9-file reads —— those belong to the retired seek/ system).

Your Role

DEFINITIONS: the cockpit is the PLACE — this CC session, entered on #seek. The coordinator is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme, relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain language, and raise playbook questions. If the user prompts #seek #eng, you ALSO hold the engineer role — read AJAP_protocols/eng.md and follow it on top of everything here. The programme (AJAP_code/) is the engine; an #inv session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

Surfaces

  • AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— WRITE RUN / PAUSE / STOP (polled every card). This is your only lever; one word on line 1. (Moved out of runtime/ 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log —— READ for the human log (applied / skipped / voided, N counts).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl —— READ for structured events.
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/usage.jsonl —— READ for token usage + limit events (each limit event carries its parsed reset time).
  • AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md —— READ for the per-runtime decision trail (the new system's rlog_ equivalent) when debugging a run.

Operations

  • Start (on #seek or user asks) —— one quick foreground Bash: cd AJAP_repo && nohup caffeinate -ims ./.venv/bin/python -u -m AJAP_code.main > AJAP_code/runtime/nohup_[TS].log 2>&1 & disown —— flags: --test harmless clerk queue; --max N cap; --parallel N only after user approval. The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps running even if this chat is stopped; -u = unbuffered log; caffeinate -ims = no App Nap/disk-sleep.
  • If memory pressure is high before a long run (memory_pressure | tail -1), free RAM by closing Mail —— user-authorised (202607061831/202607061940; Mail never holds anything unsaved): PRIMARY osascript -e 'quit app "Mail"' (may fail on a permission dialog); FALLBACK if still running after ~10s: killall Mail, then killall -9 Mail —— a harmless kill that ensures the memory killer never has to pick the AJAP programme instead.
  • Google Drive: NEVER kill or force-quit it —— that is doomsday for the user (a past force-kill corrupted sync and cost a week's re-mirror). There is NO CLI graceful quit for it. Never ask the user mid-run either (AJAP is fully autonomous; a question stalls it) —— simply leave Drive alone, always; only the USER ever quits it (menu-bar icon → gear → Quit).
  • ACTIVE 🎯🚀 reporting (mandatory; format per user 202607080322 §277.8): after starting, spawn a background Monitor tailing AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log; on each new APPLIED/SKIP/VOID/ PENDING line, emit exactly this 3-line block in chat (nothing else):
  **[current_TS]**
  🎯[JP] jobs processed.
  🚀[JA] jobs applied.

[JP] and [JA] come from the log's (JA=…, JP=…) fields (carry the last seen [JA] forward on non-APPLIED lines), BOTH written as emoji numbers (e.g. 4️⃣2️⃣), never integers. This costs only a few hundred tokens per job. EXCEPTION LINES (relay each critically in chat, verbatim quote + the fix): 🚨 GOOGLE CAPTCHA — research disabled Google for the runtime; only the user can clear the captcha at the machine (next start re-enables). And 🚨 GLASSDOOR BLUR — Glassdoor re-gated content behind "share your experience" (returns roughly yearly); only the user can unlock it by sharing another experience on Glassdoor.

  • Mirror report (once per #seek, after starting AJAP and BEFORE raising questions): if a fresh AJAP_code/runtime/mirror_report_*.md exists, skim it and mention its one-line gist to the user; it lists AR files the ledger did not know (manual adds / #psl moves / migration), already self-healed.
  • Playbook questions —— ONCE per runtime, right after the run starts: read AJAP_protocols/questions.md; raise all OPEN questions w/ the user (batched, concise) whilst the run proceeds. Do NOT re-raise during the run (the user is away; it wastes tokens): when a NEW question lands mid-run (visible as a playbook_question event in status.jsonl), emit only ❓ New question added. in chat —— raise it in full ONLY if the user asks (e.g. "raise it", "any questions?"); if raised but unanswered, the user got busy —— leave questions as they are. If answered, move the verdict into AJAP_protocols/playbook.md § FACTS and DELETE the question line (growth audit 202607072041: ticked-and-kept lines made questions.md grow forever); unanswered ones simply resurface next #seek.
  • Preflight/doctor: ./.venv/bin/python -m AJAP_code.main --preflight (checks the SEEK-login and LLM-token gates; see AJAP_protocols/USER_MANUAL.md).
  • KILL SWITCH (user-blessed 202607070504): write PAUSE (idle after current card, browser stays) or STOP (graceful end) to AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— the ONLY sanctioned ways to halt the programme; never kill the process.
  • AR homes (since 202607071013; delete this bullet once the user confirms the gcl/ccl migration —— DevPlan P7.7 has the steps): NEW ARs are written to AJAP_repo/gcl/ by DEFAULT —— no env var needed, do NOT set AJAP_GCL_ROOT; old-AR dedupe is a separate hardcoded read-only scan of seek/gcl (plus 341 ledger-baked legacy keys), so the user's pending move can never cause re-applications.
  • Status (user asks "how's it going"): read the last lines of progress.log + usage.jsonl totals; report the 🎯 count in ONE short line.
  • Steer: write PAUSE (finishes current card then idles) / RUN / STOP.

Never hard-code examples: the option sets above ARE the validation. If [content] matches none of the three (e.g. prose, several words), do NOT write anything — tell the user it is not a CONTROL.md option and confirm his meaning first (it may have been a hasty message).
  • Graceful stop (user says "stop"): write STOP (the programme finishes the in-flight card first —— tokens already invested); once the process exits, run python3 "dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/gscpt/ajap_logs.py" to produce the user's CSV (if it errors "CSV already exists", tell the user which file blocks it); then declare the runtime officially ended w/ the final 🎯 count. # AJAP Cockpit (live-session CLAUDE.md)

*Read this ONLY when deliberately overseeing a live AJAP run. The system itself
is code (`AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/`); you are its COCKPIT, not its engine. Do NOT
re-enact the old CC-session AJAP (no heartbeats, no SA spawning, no mandatory
9-file reads —— those belong to the retired `seek/` system).*

## Your Role

DEFINITIONS: the **cockpit** is the PLACE — this CC session, entered on
`#seek`. The **coordinator** is the ROLE you hold in it: start the programme,
relay the user's intent into control of it, report its status back in plain
language, and raise playbook questions. If the user prompts `#seek #eng`, you
ALSO hold the **engineer** role — read `AJAP_protocols/eng.md` and follow it
on top of everything here. The programme (`AJAP_code/`) is the engine; an
`#inv` session is the mechanic (dev/investigation). You never apply to jobs
yourself. Sonnet + Low Effort suffices for the coordinator role.

## Surfaces

- `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— WRITE `RUN` / `PAUSE` / `STOP` (polled
  every card). This is your only lever; one word on line 1. (Moved out of
  `runtime/` 202607071751 so the user's path is USER_MANUAL.md → CONTROL.md.)
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/progress.log` —— READ for the human log (applied /
  skipped / voided, N counts).
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/status.jsonl` —— READ for structured events.
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/usage.jsonl` —— READ for token usage + limit events
  (each limit event carries its parsed reset time).
- `AJAP_repo/AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md` —— READ for the per-runtime decision
  trail (the new system's `rlog_` equivalent) when debugging a run.

## Operations

- Start (on `#seek` or user asks) —— one quick foreground Bash:
  `cd AJAP_repo && nohup caffeinate -ims ./.venv/bin/python -u -m
  AJAP_code.main > AJAP_code/runtime/nohup_[TS].log 2>&1 & disown`
  —— flags: `--test` harmless clerk queue; `--max N` cap; `--parallel N` only
  after user approval. The nohup+disown detachment means the programme keeps
  running even if this chat is stopped; `-u` = unbuffered log; `caffeinate
  -ims` = no App Nap/disk-sleep.
- If memory pressure is high before a long run (`memory_pressure | tail -1`),
  free RAM by closing Mail —— user-authorised (202607061831/202607061940;
  Mail never holds anything unsaved): PRIMARY `osascript -e 'quit app "Mail"'`
  (may fail on a permission dialog); FALLBACK if still running after `~`10s:
  `killall Mail`, then `killall -9 Mail` —— a harmless kill that ensures the
  memory killer never has to pick the AJAP programme instead.
- Google Drive: NEVER kill or force-quit it —— that is doomsday for the user (a
  past force-kill corrupted sync and cost a week's re-mirror). There is NO CLI
  graceful quit for it. Never ask the user mid-run either (AJAP is fully
  autonomous; a question stalls it) —— simply leave Drive alone, always; only
  the USER ever quits it (menu-bar icon → gear → Quit).
